Ἀπελθόντες οὖν ἡμεῖς ἐκεῖ, παρεβάλομεν τῷ ἀββᾷ Ἰσαὰκ, Θηβαίῳ ὄντι τῷ γένει. Καὶ διηγήσατο ὁ γέρων λέγων, ὅτι Πρὸ τῶν πεντήκοντα δύο ἐτῶν ὡς εἰργασάμην τὸ ἐργόχειρόν μου, ἔκαμνον δὲ κονωπίωνα μέγαν, ἐπλανήθην, καὶ ἐθλιβόμην, ὅτι τὸ σφάλμα μου οὐχ εὕρισκον. Ἐποίησα οὖν ὅλην τὴν ἡμέραν ἀδολεσχῶν, καὶ οὐκ ἐγίνωσκόν τι ποιῆσαι.

And one can see the ass every time going up alone, and serving only the elders, and lending itself to no one else.

3028CHAPTER 159

Abba Menas, the superior of the monastery of abba Severian, said about abba Sophronius the grazer that he grazed about the Dead Sea, spending seventy years naked, eating only plants, and taking nothing else. He also said this about him, that he had heard him say: "I prayed the Lord that demons might not come near my cave; and I used to see them coming as far as three stades from the cave, and not able to come near."

The same Menas said to the brethren who were in the coenobium, "My little children, let us flee worldly conversations. For they know how to do harm, especially to the younger ones."

The elder said again, "Every age must repent, young and old alike, as being about to enjoy eternal life with praise and great glory: the young, because in the prime of desire they have put their necks under the yoke; the old, because they have been able to change the evil habit of mind they had grown used to over many years."

CHAPTER 160

Abba Paul, the superior of the monastery of abba Theognius, told us that a certain ascetic elder said: "One day, as I sat in my cell and was doing my handiwork (I was stitching little baskets) and saying the psalms, behold, something like a Saracen boy came in through the window, wearing a mazarion; and standing in front of me, he began to dance; and while I was saying the psalms he said to me, 'Elder, do I dance well?' I answered nothing. And again he said to me, 'Dost thou like the way I dance, elder?' So when I gave him no answer at all, he said to me, 'What dost thou think, wicked old man, that thou art doing some great thing? I tell thee, in the sixty-fifth, and in the sixty-sixth, and in the sixty-seventh, thou hast gone astray.' Then I rose and made a prostration to God; and at once he vanished."

CHAPTER 161

There is a city in the Thebaid called Lyco. Six miles from it there is a mountain. Monks live on this mountain, some in caves, some in cells. So we went there and came to abba Isaac, a Theban by race. And the elder told us: "Fifty-two years ago, as I was doing my handiwork (I was making a large mosquito net), I went wrong, and I was distressed, because I could not find my mistake. So I spent the whole day fretting, and I did not know what to do.

Καὶ ὡς ἤμην ἀδολεσχῶν, ἰδοὺ διὰ τῆς θυρίδος εἰσέρχεται νεανίσκος λέγων μοι, Ἐπλανήθης, ἀλλὰ 3029δός μοι, ἵνα διορθώσωμαι αὐτό. Λέγω αὐτῷ· ὝπαγεΑ σὺ τῶν ὧδε, καὶ μὴ γένηταί μοί ποτε. Ἀπεκρίθη μοι καὶ αὐτός· Ἀλλὰ ζημιοῖ ἑαυτὸν, ἐὰν κακῶς ποιήσης αὐτόν. Λέγω αὐτῷ· Περὶ τούτου σοὶ μὴ μελέτω. Πάλιν λέγει μοι· Ἀλλ᾿ ἐλεῶ σε, ὅτι τὸν κόπον σου ἀπόλλεις. Τότε εἶπον αὐτῷ· Κακῶς ἦλθες ὧδε μετὰ τῶν φερόντων σε. Τότε λέγει μοι· Ἀληθῶς σύ με ἠνάγκασας ὧδε ἐλθεῖν, καὶ ἐμὸς εἶ. Λέγω αὐτῷ· Πῶς τοῦτο λέγεις; Ὁ δὲ εἶπεν· Ὅτι ἔχεις τρεῖς Κυριακὰς κοινωνῶν, καὶ ἐχθραίνων τῷ γείτονί σου. Ἐγὼ δὲ εἶπον· Ψευδῆ λέγεις μοι. Καὶ αὐτός· Οὐ διὰ τὸ μάττιν τῆς φακῆς ἐμνησικάκησας αὐτῷ; Καὶ ἐγώ εἰμι ὁ ἐπὶ τῆς μνησικακίας, καὶ ἀπ᾿ ἄρτι ἐμὸς εἶ. Ὡς οὖν ἤκουσα ταῦτα ἐγὼ, ἐάσας τὸ κελλίον μου, ἀπῆλθον πρὸς τὸν ἀδελφὸν, καὶ ἔβαλον αὐτῷ μετάνοιαν, καὶ ἐποιησάμην ἀγάπην, καὶ ὑποστρέψας εὗρον, ὅτι ἔκαυσεν τὸν κονωπίωνα, καὶ τὸ ψιάθιον, ὅπου τὴν μετάνοιαν ἔβαλλον, διὰ τὸ φθονῆσαι αὐτὸν ἐπὶ τῇ ἡμετέρᾳ ἀγάπῃ.

Ἀπὸ εἴκοσι μιλίων Ἀλεξανδρείας λαύρα ἐστὶ λεγομένη τοῦ Καλαμῶνος, ἀνὰ μέσον τοῦ ὀκτωκαιδεκάτου, καὶ τοῦ Μαφορᾶ. Ἐν ταύτῃ παρεβάλομεν τῷ ἀββᾷ Θεοδώρῳ, λαβόντες καὶ τὸν σοφιστὴν Σωφρόνιον, καὶ ἐπηρωτήσαμεν αὐτὸν, λέγοντες· Καλόν ἐστι, Πάτερ, ἐὰν παραβάλωμεν πρός τινα, ἢ ἐὰν παραβάλῃ ἡμῖν τις, λύειν ἐκ τοῦ οἴνου; Ἀπεκρίθη ἡμῖν ὁ γέρων, Οὐ. Λέγομεν οὖν αὐτῷ· Καὶ πῶς οἱ ἀρχαῖοι ἔλυον Πατέρες; Ἀπεκρίθη ὁ γέρων· Οἱ ἀρχαῖοι Πάτερες ὡς μεγάλοι καὶ δυνατοὶ ἴσχυον καὶ λῦσαι καὶ πάλιν δῆσαι. Ἡ δὲ ἡμετέρα γενεὰ, τέκνον, οὐκ ἔστιν ἱκανὴ λῦσαι καὶ δῆσαι· ἀλλ᾿ ἐὰν λύσωμεν, οὐκ ἔτι κρατοῦμεν τὴν ἄσκησιν ἡμῶν.

Ἔλεγεν ὁ ἀββᾶς Ἀλέξανδρος ὁ τοῦ Καλαμῶνος τοῦ ἐν τῷ ἁγίῳ Ἰορδάνῃ, ὅτι Ἐν μιᾷ ὡς ἤμην πρὸς τὸν ἀββᾶν Παῦλον τὸν Ἑλλαδικὸν εἰς τὸ σπήλαιον αὐτοῦ, ἰδού τις ἐλθὼν, ἔκρουσε τὴν θύραν, καὶ ἐξελθὼν ὁ γέρων ἤνοιξεν αὐτῷ. Καὶ ἐξενεγκὼν ἄρτον καὶ βρεκτὰ ἔβαλεν ἔμπροσθεν αὐτοῦ, καὶ ἔτρωγεν. Ἐγὼ δὲ ὑπέλαβον, ὅτι ξένος ἐστὶ, καὶ παρακύψας διὰ τῆς θυρίδος, θεωρῶ, ὅτι λέων ἐστί. Καὶ λέγω τῷΟ γέροντι· Διατὶ, καλόγηρε, δέδωκας αὐτῷ φαγεῖν; φράσον μοι τὴν αἰτίαν. Ὁ δὲ λέγει μοι· Παρήγγειλα αὐτῷ μὴ ἀδικῆσαι μήτε ἄνθρωπον μήτε κτῆνος, καὶ, Ἔρχου ἡμερήσιον, καὶ παρέχω σοι τὴν τροφήν σου. Καὶ ἰδοὺ ἔχω ἑπτὰ μῆνας, ὅτι ἔρχεται δεύτερον τῆς ἡμέρας, καὶ παρέχω αὐτῷ τὴν τροφήν. Πάλιν μετ᾿ ὀλίγας ἡμέρας παρέβαλον αὐτῷ θέλων ἀγοράσαι παρ᾿ αὐτοῦ φλασκία. Καὶ λέγω αὐτῷ· Τί ἐστιν, καλόγηρε; Πῶς ἔχει ὁ λέων; Ἀπεκρίθη μοι, Κακῶς. Λέγω αὐτῷ, Διατί;

And as I was fretting, behold, a young man came in through the window, saying to me, 'Thou hast gone wrong; but 3029give it to me, so that I may put it right.' I said to him, 'Go away from here, and may it never be mine.' He too answered me, 'But thou harmest thyself, if thou dost it badly.' I said to him, 'Do not trouble thyself about that.' Again he said to me, 'But I pity thee, because thou art losing thy toil.' Then I said to him, 'Thou hast come here to thy hurt, with those who bring thee.' Then he said to me, 'Truly it is thou who forced me to come here, and thou art mine.' I said to him, 'How dost thou say this?' He said, 'Because for three Lord's Days thou hast been communicating while at enmity with thy neighbour.' I said, 'Thou tellest me lies.' And he: 'Didst thou not bear rancour against him over the business of the lentils[?]? And I am the one set over rancour, and from now on thou art mine.' So when I heard this, I left my cell, went off to the brother, made him a prostration, and we made our peace; and when I came back, I found that he had burnt the mosquito net, and the rush mat on which I used to make my prostrations, because he envied our love."

CHAPTER 162

Twenty miles from Alexandria there is a laura called that of Calamon, between the Eighteenth and Maphora. There we came to abba Theodore, bringing with us the sophist Sophronius too, and we asked him, "Is it good, Father, if we visit someone, or someone visits us, to break our abstinence with wine?" The elder answered us, "No." So we said to him, "Then how did the ancient Fathers break it?" The elder answered, "The ancient Fathers, being great and strong, had strength both to loose and to bind again. But our generation, child, is not able to loose and bind; if we loose, we no longer keep hold of our ascesis."

CHAPTER 163

Abba Alexander, of the Calamon on the holy Jordan, said: "One day, as I was with abba Paul the Helladic in his cave, behold, someone came and knocked at the door; and the elder went out and opened to him. And bringing out bread and soaked pulse, he set them before him, and he ate. I supposed that he was a stranger, and peeping through the window, I saw that it was a lion. And I said to the elder, 'Why, good elder, hast thou given him to eat? Tell me the reason.' He said to me, 'I charged him not to harm man or beast, and said, "Come every day, and I give thee thy food." And see, for seven months now he comes twice a day, and I give him his food.'

"Again, after a few days, I visited him, wanting to buy flasks from him. And I said to him, 'How is it, good elder? How is the lion?' He answered me, 'Badly.' I said to him, 'Why?'

Ὁδὲ λέγει μοι, Τῇ παρελθούσῃ ἡμέρᾳ ἦλθεν ὧδε, ἵνα παράσχω αὐτῷ φαγεῖν, καὶ θεωρῶ ὅλην τὴν γενειάδα αὐτοῦ ἡμαγμένην, 3032καὶ λέγω αὐτῷ·Τί ἐστι; Παρήκουσάς μου, καὶ σαρκὸς ἔφαγες. Εὐλογητὸς Κύριος, οὐκ ἔτι παρέχω σοι τὴν βρῶσιν τῶν Πατέρων, σαρκοφάγε. Ὕπαγε ἔνθεν. Ὁ δὲ, οὐκ ἤθελεν ἀπελθεῖν. Τότε λαβὼν σχοινίον, καὶ τριπλώσας αὐτὸ, παρέσχον αὐτῷ τρεῖς πληγὰς, καὶ ἀπῆλθεν.

Ἀδελφὸς παρέβαλεν τῷ ἀββᾷ Βίκτορι τῷ ἡσυχαστῇ εἰς τὴν λαύραν Ἐλούσης, καὶ λέγει αὐτῷ· Τί ποιήσω, Πάτερ, ὅτι κατακυριεύει μου τὸ πάθος τῆς ὀλιγωρίας; Ἀπεκρίθη ὁ γέρων· Ἀῤῥωστία ψυχῆς ἐστιν. Ὥσπερ γὰρ οἱ ὀφθαλμιῶντες, ἐπειδὴ μᾶλλον πάσχωσιν, πλέον φῶς δοκοῦσιν βλέπειν, οἱ δὲ ὑγιαίνοντες, ὀλίγον· οὕτως καὶ οἱ ὀλιγόψυχοι, ὑπὸ μικρᾶς ὀλιγωρίας ταχέως ἐξάπτονται, καὶ νομίζουσιν πολλὴν εἶναι τὴν ὀλιγωρίαν, οἱ δὲ ὑγιαίνοντες τὴν ψυχὴν, μᾶλλον ἐν τοῖς πειρασμοῖς χαίρουσιν.

Διηγήσατο ἡμῖν ὁ φιλόχριστος περί τινος ληστοῦ ὀνόματι Κυριακοῦ, ὅτι ἐλήστευεν περὶ τὰ μέρη Ἐμαοῦς, ἥτις ἐστὶν Νικόπολις· καὶ τοιούτως γέγονεν πικρὸς, καὶ ἀπάνθρωπος, ὅτι λύκον αὐτὸν ἐκάλουν. Εἶχεν δὲ σὺν αὐτῷ καὶ ἄλλους λῃστεύοντας οὐ μόνον Χριστιανοὺς, ἀλλὰ καὶ Ἰουδαίους καὶ Σαμαρείτας. Ἐν μιᾷ οὖν ἦλθον ἀπὸ κτήματος τῶν μερῶν Νικοπόλεώς τινες εἰς τὴν ἁγίαν πόλιν τῇ μεγάλῃ ἑβδομάδι φωτίσαι τὰ παιδία αὐτῶν. Ὡς οὖν ἐφώτισαν καὶ ὑπέστρεφον εἰς τὸ ἴδιον κτῆμα, ἵνα ποιήσωσιν τὴν ἁγίαν ἀναστάσιμον ἡμέραν, ὑπήντησαν οὖν αὐτοῖς οἱ λῃσταὶ οὐ παρόντος τοῦ ἀρχιληστοῦ. Καὶ οἱ μεν ἄνδρες φυγῇ ἐχρήσαντο· τὰς δὲ γυναῖκας κρατήσαντες, καὶ ῥίψαντες τὰ νεοφώτιστα, οἱ Ἑβραῖοι καὶ οἱ Σαμαρεῖται, ἔσχον αὐτάς. Ὡς οὖν οἱ ἄνδρες ἔφυγον, ὑπήντησεν αὐτοῖς ἀρχιληστὴς, καὶ ἐπηρώτησεν αὐτοὺς, Τί φεύγετε; Οἱ δὲ εἶπον αὐτῷ τὸ συμβὰν αὐτοῖς. Λαβὼν οὖν αὐτοὺς ἦλθεν πρὸς τοὺς ἑταίρους αὐτοῦ, καὶ εὗρεν τὰ παιδία ἐῤῥιμμένα εἰς τὴν γῆν. Καὶ μαθὼν τίς ἐποίησεν, ἀπεκεφάλισεν τοὺς πεποιηκότας τὸ μύσος, καὶ ποιήσας τοὺς ἄνδρας λαβεῖν τὰ παιδία, τῷ μὴ ἀνασχεθῆναι τὰς γυναῖκας ψαῦσαι διὰ τὸν ἐπ᾿ αὐταῖς μολυσμὸν, διέσωσεν αὐτοὺς ὁ ἀρχιληστὴς ἕως τοῦ κτήματος αὐτῶν. Μετὰ οὖν ὀλίγον χρόνον ἐκρατήθη ὁ ἀρχιλῃστὴς, καὶ ἐποίησεν ἐν τῇ φυλακῇ δέκα χρόνους, καὶ οὐδεὶς τῶν ἀρχόντων ἐφόνευσεν αὐτόν. Ὕστερον δὲ καὶ ἀπελύθη. Ἔλεγεν οὖν ἀεὶ, ὅτι διὰ τοὺς παῖδας ἐκείνους τὸν πικρὸν ἔφυγον θάνατον. Ἔβλεπον γὰρ αὐτοὺς κατ᾿ ὄναρ λέγοντάς μοι, Μὴ φοβοῦ, ἡμεῖς ὑπὲρ σοῦ ἀπολογούμεθα. Συνετύχομεν δὲ αὐτῷ ἐγὼ καὶ ὁ ἀββᾶς Ἰωάννης ὁ πρεσβύτερος τοῦ κοινοβίου τῶν Εὐνούχων· καὶ τὰ αὐτὰ ἡμῖν διηγήσατο, καὶ ἐδοξάσαμεν τὸν Θεόν.

He said to me, 'Yesterday he came here for me to give him something to eat, and I see his whole beard bloodied, 3032and I say to him, "What is this? Thou hast disobeyed me, and eaten flesh. Blessed be the Lord, I no longer give thee the food of the Fathers, flesh-eater. Go away from here." But he would not go away. Then I took a rope, folded it three times, and gave him three strokes, and he went away.'

CHAPTER 164

A brother visited abba Victor the solitary at the Laura of Elusa, and said to him: "What shall I do, Father? For the passion of faintheartedness lords it over me." The elder answered: "It is a sickness of the soul. For just as people with sore eyes, the more they suffer, think they see more light, while the healthy see little; so too the faint of soul are quickly set alight by a little faintheartedness, and think the faintheartedness great; but those who are healthy in soul rejoice the more in temptations."

CHAPTER 165

The Christ-loving man told us about a robber named Cyriacus, that he used to rob around Emmaus, which is Nicopolis; and he became so savage and inhuman that they called him the Wolf. He had with him others robbing too, not only Christians but also Jews and Samaritans. One day some people came from an estate in the district of Nicopolis to the holy city in Great Week, to have their children illumined. When they had had them illumined and were going back to their own estate to keep the holy day of the Resurrection, the robbers met them, the chief robber not being there. The men took to flight; but the Hebrews and the Samaritans seized the women, threw down the newly illumined, and had them. As the men were fleeing, the chief robber met them and asked them, "Why are you fleeing?" They told him what had happened to them. So he took them and came to his companions, and found the children thrown on the ground. And learning who had done it, he beheaded those who had done the abomination; and he made the men take up the children, since they could not bear to touch the women because of the defilement upon them; and the chief robber brought them safe as far as their estate. A little while after, the chief robber was caught, and he spent ten years in prison, and none of the governors put him to death. Afterwards he was even released. And he used always to say: "Because of those children I escaped a bitter death. For I used to see them in my sleep, saying to me, 'Fear not; we are pleading for thee.'" Abba John, the priest of the coenobium of the Eunuchs, and I met him, and he told us the same things, and we glorified God.

Ἔλεγεν ὁ ἀββᾶς Σαββάτιος· Ὡς ἐκαθήμην εἰς τὴν λαύραν τοῦ ἀββᾶ Φιρμίνου, ἦλθέν τις λῃστὴς πρὸς 3033τὸν ἀββᾶν Ζώσιμον τὸν Κίλικα, καὶ παρεκάλει τὸν γέροντα, λέγων, Ποίησον ἀγάπην διὰ τὸν Θεὸν, ἐπειδὴ πολλῶν φόνων κύριος ὑπάρχω· ποίησόν με μοναχὸν, ἵνα τὸ λοιπὸν ἡσυχάσω ἐκ τῶν κακῶν μου. Ὁ δὲ γέρων νουθετήσας αὐτὸν ἐποίησεν αὐτὸν μοναχὸν, παρασχὼν καὶ τὸ ἅγιον σχῆμα. Μετ᾿ ὀλίγας μὲν οὖν ἡμέρας λέγει αὐτῷ ὁ γέρων, Πίστευσόν μοι, τέκνον, ὅτι ὧδε μεῖναι οὐ δύνασαι· εἰ γὰρ ἀκούσει ἄρχων, κρατήσει· ὁμοίως δὲ καὶ οἱ ἀντιφαντιασταί σου ἐλθόντες φονεύσουσίν σε· ἀλλ᾿ ἄκουσόν μου, καὶ ἀποφέρω σε εἰς τὸ κοινόβιον ἀπέχον τῶν ὧδε. Καὶ ἀπήνεγκεν αὐτὸν εἰς τὸ κοινόβιον τοῦ ἀββᾶ Δωροθέου, πλησίον Γάζης καὶ τοῦ Μαιουμά. Καὶ ποιήσας χρόνους ἐννέα, καὶ μαθὼν τὸ Ψαλτήριον καὶ ἅπασαν τὴν μοναχικὴν κατάστασιν, ἀνέρχεται πάλιν εἰς τὰ Φιρμίνου πρὸς τὸν γέροντα, καὶ λέγει αὐτῷ, Κύριε ἀββᾶ, ποίησον ἔλεος μετ᾿ ἐμοῦ, καὶ δός μοι τὰ ἱμάτιά μου, τὰ κοσμικὰ, καὶ λάβε μοναστικά. Ὁ δὲ γέρων λυπηθεὶς λέγει πρὸς αὐτὸν, Διὰ τί, τέκνον; Ἀπεκρίθη λέγων· Ἰδοὺ ἐννέα, ὡς οἶδας, Πάτερ, ἔτη ἔχω εἰς τὸ κοινόβιον, καὶ ὡς ἐπὶ μοὶ ἐνήστευσα, καὶ ἐγκρατευσάμην, καὶ μετὰ πάσης ἡσυχίας καὶ φόβου Θεοῦ ἔζησα ἐν ὑποταγῇ, καὶ ἐπίσταμαι, ὅτι αὐτοῦ ἀγαθότης συνεχώρησέν μοι τὰ πολλά μου κακὰ, πλὴν θεωρῶ ἑκάστοτε παῖδα λέγοντά μοι· Διατί με ἐφόνευσας; Τοῦτον θεωρῶ κατὰ τοὺς ὕπνους, καὶ ἐν τῇ ἐκκλησίᾳ, καὶ ἐν τῷ ἀριστηρίῳ ταῦτά μοι λέγοντα, καὶ μίαν ὥραν οὐκ ἐνδίδωσί μοι. Διὰ τοῦτο οὖν, Πάτερ, θέλω ἀπελθεῖν, ἵνα ὑπὲρ τοῦ παιδὸς ἀποθάνω. Μάτην γὰρ ἐφόνευσα τὸ παιδίον. Λαβὼν οὖν τὰ ἱμάτια, καὶ φορέσας ἐξῆλθεν ἐκ τῆς λαύρας, καὶ ὑπάγει εἰς τὴν Διόσπολιν, καὶ τῇ ἑξῆς ἡμέρᾳ κρατηθεὶς ἀπεκεφαλίσθη.

Ἔλεγεν ὁ ἀββᾶς Ἀγαθόνικος ἡγούμενος τοῦ κοινοβίου τοῦ καστελλίου τοῦ ὁσίου Πατρὸς ἡμῶν Σαβὰ, ὅτι Ἐν μᾇ κατῆλθον εἰς τὴν Ῥουβὰν παραβαλεῖν τῷ ἀββᾷ Ποιμένι τῷ βοσκῷ. Καὶ δὴ εὑρὼν αὐτὸν, εἶπον αὐτῷ τοὺς λογισμούς μου. Ὡς οὖν ὀψία γέγονεν εἴασέ με εἰς σπήλαιον. Ἦν δὲ χειμὼν, καὶ γέγονε κατ᾿ ἐκείνην τὴν νύκτα κρύος μέγα, καὶ πάνυ ἐρίγωσα. Ἐλθὼν οὖν τῷ πρωῒ γέρων λέγει μοι, Τί ἐστι, τέκνον; Λέγω αὐτῷ, Συγχώρησόν μοι, Πάτερ, χαλεπὴν ἐποίησα νύκτα ἐκ τοῦ κρύους.Λέγει μοι καὶ αὐτὸς, Φύσει, τέκνον; Ἐγὼ οὐκ ἐρίγωσα. Ἐθαύμασα δὲ ἐγὼ ἀκούσας· ἦν γὰρ γυμνός. Καὶ λέγω αὐτῷ· Ποίησον ἀγάπην, πῶς οὐκ ἐρίγωσας; Ὁ δὲ λέγει· Λέων ἐλθὼν, πλησίον μου κεκοίμηται, καὶ αὐτός με ἐθέρμανεν. Πλὴν λέγω σοι, ἀδελφὲ, ὑπὸ θηρίων ἔχω βρωθῆναι. Λέγω αὐτῷ· Διὰ τί; Ὁ δὲ λέγει μοι, Ὅτι εἰς τὴν χώραν ἡμῶν ὅτε ἤμην (ἦσαν δὲ καὶ οἱ ἀμφότεροι Γαλάται), ποιμὴν προβάτων ἤμην, καὶ παρεῖδον ξένον παροδίτην, καὶ ἔφαγον αὐτὸν τὰ κυνάρια τὰ ἐμὰ, καὶ δυνάμενος σῶσαι αὐτὸν, οὐκ ἔσωσα, ἀλλ᾿ εἴασα αὐτὸν, καὶ ἀπέκτειναν αὐτὸν οἱ κύνες. Καὶ οἶδα, ὅτι κἀγὼ οὕτως ἔχω ἀποθανεῖν.

CHAPTER 166

Abba Sabbatius used to say: "When I was living in the laura of abba Firminus, a robber came to 3033abba Zosimus the Cilician, and besought the elder, saying, 'Do me a kindness, for God's sake, since I am guilty of many murders: make me a monk, so that from now on I may rest from my evil deeds.' The elder admonished him and made him a monk, giving him also the holy habit. A few days later the elder said to him, 'Believe me, child, thou canst not stay here; for if the governor hears of it, he will arrest thee; and likewise thy adversaries [?] will come and kill thee. But listen to me, and I will take thee off to a coenobium far from here.' And he took him to the coenobium of abba Dorotheus, near Gaza and Maiuma. And he spent nine years there, and learned the Psalter and the whole order of the monastic life; and he came up again to Firminus's, to the elder, and said to him, 'Lord abba, have mercy on me, and give me my clothes, my worldly ones, and take the monastic ones.' The elder was grieved and said to him, 'Why, child?' He answered, saying, 'Behold, as thou knowest, Father, I have nine years in the coenobium; and as far as in me lay I fasted, and kept self-control, and lived in subjection with all stillness and fear of God; and I know that His goodness has forgiven me my many evils. But every time I see a boy saying to me, "Why didst thou kill me?" I see him in my sleep, and in church, and in the refectory, saying these things to me, and he does not give me one hour's peace. So for this reason, Father, I want to go, so that I may die for the boy. For I killed the child for nothing.' So he took the clothes and put them on, and went out of the laura, and went off to Diospolis; and the next day he was arrested and beheaded."

CHAPTER 167

Abba Agathonicus, superior of the coenobium of the Castellium of our holy father Sabas, used to say: "One day I went down to the Ruba to visit abba Poemen the Grazer. And when I found him, I told him my thoughts. When evening came, he left me in a cave. It was winter, and that night there was a great frost, and I was bitterly cold. So in the morning the elder came and said to me, 'What is it, child?' I said to him, 'Forgive me, Father, I had a hard night from the cold.' And he said to me, 'Truly, child, I was not cold.' I was amazed to hear it, for he was naked. And I said to him, 'Do me a kindness: how wast thou not cold?' He said, 'A lion came and slept beside me, and it warmed me. But I tell thee, brother, I am to be eaten by wild beasts.' I said to him, 'Why?' He said to me, 'Because when I was in our country' (both of them were Galatians), 'I was a shepherd of sheep, and I overlooked a stranger passing by, and my dogs ate him; and though I could have saved him, I did not save him, but let him be, and the dogs killed him. And I know that I too must die so.'"

Καὶ μετὰ τρεῖς χρόνους ἐβρώθη ὑπὸ θηρίων κατὰ τὸν λόγον αὐτοῦ ὁ γέρων.

3036Ἔλεγεν ὁ ἀββᾶς Ἀλέξανδρος ὁ γέρων, ὁ τοῦ ἀββᾶ Βικεντίου, τοῖς ἀδελφοῖς· Οἱ Πατέρες ἡμῶν ἐδίωκον τὰς ἐρήμους καὶ τὰς θλίψεις· ἡμεῖς δὲ διώκομεν τὰς πόλεις καὶ τὴν ἄνεσιν. Εἶπεν πάλιν ὁ γέρων· Ἐπὶ τῶν Πατέρων ἡμῶν, αὗται αἱ ἀρεταὶ ἤκμασαν, ἡ ἀκτημοσύνη, καὶ ἡ ταπείνωσις· σήμερον δὲ πολιτεύεται ἡ φιλοχρηματία al. πολυκτημοσύνη. καὶ ἡ ὑπερηφανία. Εἶπεν πάλιν· Οἱ Πατέρες ἡμῶν οὐδέπω τὴν ὄψιν αὐτῶν ἔνιπτον· ἡμεῖς δὲ καὶ τὰ λουτρὰ τὰ δημόσια ἀνοίγομεν. Εἶπεν πάλιν ὁ γέρων· Οὐαί μοι, τέκνα, πολιτείαν ἀγγελικὴν ἠφανίσαμεν. Λέγει αὐτῷ ὁ μαθητὴς αὐτοῦ ὁ ἀββᾶς Βικέντιος· Ἀσθενεῖς ἐσμεν ἀληθῶς, Πάτερ. Λέγει ὁ γέρων· Τί λέγεις Βικέντιε, Ἀσθενεῖς ἐσμεν; Πίστευέ μοι, τέκνον, ὡς Ὀλυμπικοί al. παγκρατιασταὶ καὶ πύκται. ἐσμεν τῷ σώματι, ἀλλ᾿ ἡ ψυχὴ ἡμῶν ἐστιν ἡ ἀσθενοῦσα. Εἶπεν πάλιν· Φαγεῖν καὶ πιεῖν πολλὰ, καὶ φορέσαι καλῶς δυνάμεθα· ἐγκρατεύσασθαι δὲ καὶ ταπεινοφρονῆσαι οὐ δυνάμεθα. Εἶπεν πάλιν ὁ γέρων· Οὐαί σοι, Ἀλέξανδρε, οὐαί σοι· πῶς ἔχεις καταισχυνθῆναι, ὅταν ἄλλοι στεφανοῦνται!

Ἦν τις γέρων ἀπὸ ὀμμάτων εἰς τὴν Σκῆτιν, ἐν τῇ λαύρᾳ τοῦ ἀββᾶ Σισοϊ· ἀπεῖχεν δὲ τὸ κελλίον αὐτοῦ ἐκ τοῦ φρέατος ὡσεὶ δίμοιρον μιλίου. Καὶ οὐκ ἠνέσχετό ποτε, ἵνα ἄλλος αὐτῷ γεμίσει ὕδωρ· ἀλλὰ ποιήσας σχοινίον, τὴν μίαν αὐτοῦ ἀρχὴν εἰς τὸ φρέαρ ἔδησεν, καὶ τὴν ἄλλην εἰς τὸ κελλίον αὐτοῦ. καὶ ἔκειτο εἰς τὴν γῆν. Ὅτε οὖν ὑπῆγεν γεμίσαι ἑαυτῷ ὕδωρ ἐπάνω τοῦ σχοινίου περιεπάτει. Τοῦτο δὲ ὁ γέρων ἐποίησεν, ἵνα κατ᾿ αὐτοῦ τοῦ φρέατος εὐρίσκηται. Ὅτε οὖν ἡ ψάμμος ἐκ τοῦ ἀνέμου, ἐκάλυψεν τὸ σχοινίον, ἐκράτει τῇ χειρὶ τὸ σχοινίον, καὶ ἐκτίνασσεν αὐτὸ, καὶ ἐτίθη αὐτὸ πάλιν εἰς τὴν γῆν, καὶ ἐπάνω αὐτοῦ περιεπάτει. Ἀδελφὸς οὖν παρεκάλεσεν τὸν γέροντα, ἵνα φέρῃ αὐτῷ ὕδωρ. Καὶ ἀπεκρίθη ὁ γέρων· Φύσει, ἀδελφὲ, ἔχω οὕτως γεμίζων ἐμαυτῷ ἔτη εἰκοσιδύο, καὶ θέλεις τὸν κόπον μου λαβεῖν;

And three years later the elder was eaten by wild beasts, according to his word.

3036CHAPTER 168

Abba Alexander the elder, abba Vincent's elder, used to say to the brethren: "Our fathers pursued the deserts and afflictions; but we pursue the cities and ease."

The elder said again: "In the days of our fathers these virtues flourished, poverty and lowliness; but today love of money and pride hold sway."

He said again: "Our fathers never once washed their faces; but we even open the public baths."

The elder said again: "Woe is me, children, we have wiped out an angelic way of life." His disciple, abba Vincent, said to him: "We are truly weak, Father." The elder said: "What sayest thou, Vincent? 'We are weak'? Believe me, child, in body we are like Olympic athletes; it is our soul that is weak."

He said again: "We can eat and drink much and dress well; but we cannot keep self-control and be humble."

The elder said again: "Woe to thee, Alexander, woe to thee: how shalt thou be put to shame, when others are crowned!"

CHAPTER 169

There was an elder in Scetis, blind in his eyes, in the laura of abba Sisoes; and his cell was about two-thirds of a mile from the well. And he never allowed anyone else to fill water for him; but he made a rope, and tied one end of it to the well and the other to his cell, and it lay on the ground. So when he went to fill water for himself, he walked along on top of the rope. The elder did this so that he would come out at the well itself. When the sand from the wind covered the rope, he would take hold of the rope with his hand and shake it out, and lay it again on the ground, and walk along on top of it. So a brother begged the elder to let him bring him water. And the elder answered: "Truly, brother, I have been filling water for myself like this for twenty-two years, and dost thou want to take my toil from me?"

CHAPTER 170

There is a monastery some distance from Jerusalem [?].

Μοναστήριόν ἐστιν ἀπέχον ἀπὸ Ἱεροσολύμων., δύο τῶν Πατέρων ἀπῆλθον ἐπὶ τὸ Σινὰ ὄρος, εὐχῆς 3037ἕνεκα. Καὶ ἐπανελθόντες ἐν τῷ μοναστηρίῳ διηγή-Α σαντο ἡμῖν, λέγοντες· Μετὰ τὸ προσκυνῆσαι ἡμᾶς εἰς τὸ ἅγιον ὄρος, ἐπανερχομένοις συνέβη κατὰ τὴν ἔρημον πλανηθῆναι· καὶ ἐφερόμεθα ὡς ἐπὶ πελάγους ἐν τῇ ἐρήμῳ ἐπὶ πολλὰς ἡμέρας. Ἐν μιᾷ οὖν θεωροῦμεν μικρὸν σπήλαιον μακρόθεν, καὶ ὡρμήσαμεν ἐπ᾿ αὐτό. Πλησιάσαντες δὲ τῷ σπηλαίῳ, θεωροῦμεν τερὶ αὐτὸ πηγάδιον πάνυ μικρὸν, καὶ περὶ τὸ πηγάδιον ὀλίγα βοτάνια, καὶ ἴχνη ἀνθρώπου· καὶ λέγομεν πρὸς ἑαυτοὺς, ὅτι Ἀληθῶς ὧδε Θεοῦ δοῦλός ἐστιν. Εἰσελθόντες οὖν, οὐδένα ἐθεωρήσαμεν, μόνον δὲ ἠκούομέν τινος βρυχομένου. Ὡς οὖν ἐπὶ πολὺ ἐζητήσαμεν τόπον, εὑρίσκομεν ὡς ἐν τάξει φάτνης, καί τινα ἀνακείμενον ἐπ᾿ αὐτῷ· καὶ πλησιάσαντες πρὸς τὸν δοῦλον τοῦ Θεοῦ, παρεκαλοῦμεν αὐτῷ λαλῆσαι ἡμῖν. Ὡς οὖν οὐκ ἀπεκρίθη ἡμῖν, τότε κρατήσαμεν αὐτόν. Καὶ τὸ μὲν σῶμα αὐτοῦ θερμὸν ἦν ἔτι, ἡ δὲ ψυχὴ αὐτοῦ πρὸς Κύριον ἀπεληλύθει. Τότε ἔγνωμεν ὅτι ἐν τῷ εἰσελθεῖν ἡμᾶς εἰς τὸ σπήλαιον, ἀνεπάη. Λαβόντες οὖν τὸ σῶμα αὐτοῦ ἐκ τοῦ τόπου ὅπου ἔκειτο, ὡρύξαμεν ἐν αὐτῷ τῷ σπηλαίῳ, καὶ εἷς ἐξ ἡμῶν, ὃ περιβέβλητο παλλίον ἀφελόμενος, εἰς αὐτὸ ἐνετυλίξαμεν τὸ σῶμα τοῦ γέροντος, καὶ ἐν αὐτῷ τοῦτο ἐκηδεύομεν. Ηὕρομεν [δὲ] ὅτι γυνὴ ὑπῆρχεν. Καὶ ἐδοξάσαμεν τὸν Θεόν. Καὶ ποιήσαντες τὸν κανόνα ἐπάνω αὐτῆς, ἐθάψαμεν αὐτήν. Ἐν Ἀλεξανδρείᾳ δύο ἦσαν ἄνδρες θαυμαστοὶ καὶ ἐνάρετοι, ὁ ἀββᾶς Θεόδωρος ὁ φιλοσόφος, καὶ Ζώϊλος ὁ ἀναγνώστης· ἡμεῖς δὲ καὶ ἀμφοτέρων πολλὴν ἐσχήκαμεν συνήθειαν· τοῦ μὲν διὰ τὰ μαθήματα, τοῦ δὲ διὰ τὸ κοινὴν ἔχειν τὴν πατρίδα καὶ τὴν ἀναστροφήν. Καὶ ὁ μὲν ἀββᾶς Θεόδωρος ὁ φιλόσοφος, οὐδὲν ἐκέκτητο, εἰ μὴ στιχαροφελόνιον καὶ ὀλίγα πάνυ βιβλία, ἐκοιμᾶτο δὲ ἐν σκαμνίῳ εἰς οἵαν ὥραν εὕρισκεν ἐκκλησίαν· ὕστερον δὲ καὶ ἀπετάξατο εἰς τὸ τοῦ Σαμαλᾶ κοινόβιον, κἀκεῖσε τὸν βίον ἐπλήρωσεν. Ὁ δὲ ἀναγνώστης ὁ Ζώϊλος, καὶ αὐτὸς τὴν ἴσην εἶχεν ἀκτημοσύνην. Οὐδὲν γὰρ οὐδὲ οὗτος ἐκέκτητο, εἰ μὴ στιχαροφελόνιον, καὶ αὐτὸ πάνυ παλαιὸν, καὶ ὀλίγα βιβλία· ἐσχόλαζεν δὲ εἰς τὰ καλιγραφία(. Καὶ αὐτὸς δὲ τελειωθεὶς ἐν Κυρίῳ ἐτάφη εἰς τὸν Λιθαζόμενον, ἐν τῷ μοναστηρίῳ τοῦ ἀββᾶ Παλαδίου. Περὶ τούτου τοῦ Ἀββᾶ Θεοδώρου τοῦ φιλοσόφου, καὶ τοῦ ἀναγνώστου Ζωΐλου, παραβαλόντες τινὲς τῶν Πατέρων τῷ κυρῷ Κοσμᾷ τῷ σχολαστικῷ, ἠρώτησαν αὐτὸν, λέγοντες· Τίς τῶν ἀμφοτέρων μᾶλλον ἐν τοῖς τῆς ἀσκήσεως πόνοις ἐνεκαρτέρησεν;

Two of the fathers went to Mount Sinai 3037to pray. And when they came back to the monastery they told us, saying: "After we had worshipped at the holy mountain, it befell us on the way back to lose our way in the desert; and we were carried along in the desert as on the open sea for many days. One day we saw a little cave far off, and we made for it. Drawing near the cave, we saw by it a very small spring, and around the spring a few herbs, and the tracks of a man; and we said to ourselves, 'Truly a servant of God is here.' So we went in and saw no one; only we heard someone groaning. After we had searched the place a long while, we found something like a manger, and someone lying in it; and drawing near the servant of God, we begged him to speak to us. When he did not answer us, we took hold of him. And his body was still warm, but his soul had departed to the Lord. Then we knew that as we were entering the cave he had gone to his rest. So we took his body from the place where it lay, and we dug a grave in the cave itself; and one of us took off the pallium he had on, and we wrapped the elder's body in it, and in it we laid him out for burial. And we found that it was a woman. And we glorified God. And having said the office over her, we buried her."

CHAPTER 171

In Alexandria there were two wonderful and virtuous men, abba Theodore the philosopher and Zoilus the reader; and we had much acquaintance with both of them: with the one on account of studies, with the other because we had a common homeland and way of life. Abba Theodore the philosopher owned nothing but a tunic-cloak and a very few books, and he slept on a bench in whatever church he happened on at the hour. Later he renounced the world at the coenobium of Samala, and there he finished his life. Zoilus the reader too had the same poverty. For neither did he own anything but a tunic-cloak, and that a very old one, and a few books; and he gave his time to fine copying. And he too, perfected in the Lord, was buried at Lithazomenon, in the monastery of abba Palladius.

About this abba Theodore the philosopher and Zoilus the reader, some of the fathers visited master Cosmas the scholasticus and asked him, saying: "Which of the two held out more in the labours of ascesis?"

Ὁ δὲ πρὸς αὐτοὺς ἔφη· Κοινὰ μὲν ἐπ᾿ ἀμφοῖν τὰ ἐδέσματα, τὰ στρώματα, τὰ ἐσθήματα, καὶ ἡ πάντων τῶν περιττῶν 3040ἀποχὴ, καὶ ἡ ταπείνωσις, καὶ ἡ ἀκτημοσύνη, καὶ ἡ αὐτάρκεια. Ἀλλ᾿ ὁ ἀββᾶς Θεόδωρος ὁ φιλόσοφος, ἀνυπέδετος, ἐν ἀσθενείᾳ πολλῇ τῶν ὀφθαλμῶν, τὴν παλαιὰν καὶ νέαν ἐξέμαθεν· πλὴν ἔσχεν παραμυθίαν τὴν τῶν ἀδελφῶν συνδιαίτησιν, καὶ τῶν φίλων ἔντευξιν, καὶ ἀπαγωγὴν πολλὴν, ὅτε ἔπραττεν, καὶ ὅτε ἐξηγεῖτο. Τοῦ δὲ ἀναγνώστου Ζωΐλου, οὐ ξενιτεία μόνον ἐπαίνου ἀξία, ἀλλὰ καὶ ἡ ἐρημία, καὶ ὁ ἄμετρος κόπος, καὶ ἡ τοῦ στόματος φυλακὴ, καὶ ὅτι ἄφιλος, ἀνίδιος, ἀσυναλάλητος, ἐν πάσῃ κοσμικῇ ἀπραξίᾳ, καὶ πρὸς οὐδεμίαν παραθυμίαν ἐκδέδωκεν, καὶ μηδὲ ψιλῆς παρ᾿ οὐδενὸς ὑπηρεσίας ἀνεχόμενος· αὐτὸς ἥψει, αὐτὸς ἔπλυνεν, μηδὲ τὴν ἐκ τῶν ἀναγνωσμάτων ἔχων παραψυχήν· ἕτοιμός τε καὶ ἄλλοις ὑπηρετεῖν, μηδεμίαν ἔχων φροντίδα κρύους ἢ θάλπους ἢ ἀῤῥωστίας σωματικῆς, καθαρεύων ἀεὶ καὶ γέλωτος καὶ στυγνίας καὶ ἀκηδίας καὶ διαχύσεως, πρὸς τῇ τοῦ ἱματίου τραχύτητι, καὶ ὑπὸ πλήθους φθειρῶν ἀταπείνωτος ἀναλισκόμενος. Ἀλλ᾿ ἔσχεν καὶ οὗτος παρὰ τὸν πρῶτον, ἐκ τοῦ προϊέναι οὐ μετρίαν ψυχαγωγίαν, πᾶσαν ἄδειαν ἔχων εἰς παννύχιον καὶ πανημέριον πρόοδον· ἀλλὰ καὶ ταύτην τὴν ἄδειαν ἡ ἀμετρία τοῦ κόπου παρεκρούετο, καὶ πολλῇ τῇ συναναστροφῇ τὸ ἐξῃρημένον πρὸς κοσμικοὺς ἐπεδείκνυτο. Ἕκαστος οὖν τὸν ἴδιον μισθὸν λήψεται, κατὰ τὸν ἴδιον κόπον, καὶ κατὰ τὰ μέτρα τῆς ἀναστάσεως, καὶ τῆς νοερᾶς καὶ τῆς κατὰ νοῦν καθαρότητος, καὶ φόβου Θεοῦ, καὶ ἀγάπης, καὶ λειτουργίας, καὶ κατανύξεως, καὶ ἀμετεωρίστου ψαλμῳδίας τε καὶ εὐχῆς, καὶ τῆς ἐκτενοῦς πίστεως, καὶ τῆς κεκρυμμένης ἀπὸ ἀνθρώπων καὶ λεληθυίας

εὐαρεστήσεως τοῦ Θεοῦ. Περὶ τούτου δὲ κυροῦ Κοσμᾶ τοῦ σχολαστικοῦ, πολλοὶ μὲν πολλὰ, ἄλλοι δὲ ἄλλα, καὶ πλεῖστοι πλεῖστα ἡμῖν διηγήσαντο. Ὧν δὲ ἡμεῖς αὐτόπται ἦμεν, καὶ οἷς ἀκριβῶς παρηκολουθήσαμεν, ταῦτα γράφομεν, πρὸς τὴν τῶν ἐντυγχανόντων ὠφέλειαν. Ἦν γὰρ ὁ ἀνὴρ ταπεινόφρων, ἐλεήμων, ἐγκρατὴς, παρθένος, ἥσυχος, ἀόργητος, φιλοέταιρος, φιλόξενος, φιλόπτωχος. Πολλὴν οὖν ἡμῖν ὁ θαυμάσιος οὗτος ἀνὴρ ὠφέλειαν ἐνεποίει, οὐ μόνον θεωρούμενος καὶ διδάσκων, ἀλλὰ καὶ διὰ τὸ εἶναιαὐτὸν πολύβιβλον ὑπὲρ πάντας τοὺς ἐν Ἀλεξανδρείᾳ ὄντας, καὶ προθύμως παρεῖχεν τοῖς θέλουσιν. Ἦν δὲ καὶ ἀκτήμων· ἐν ὅλῳ γὰρ τῷ οἴκῳ αὐτοῦ, ἕτερον οὐδὲν ἦν θεωρῆσαι, εἰ μὴ βιβλία, καὶ κλίνην, καὶ τράπεζαν. Ἐξὸν δὲ ἦν παντὶ ἀνθρώπῳεἰσιέναι, καὶ ἐπερωτᾶν τὰ πρὸς ὠφέλειαν, καὶ ἀναγινώσκειν. Καθ᾿ ἑκάστην δὲ ἡμέραν εἰσερχόμην πρὸς αὐτὸν, καὶ ὡς ἐπὶ τῆς ἀληθείας, οὐδέποτε εἰσῆλθον, καὶ οὐχ εὗρον αὐτὸν ἢ ἀναγινώσκοντα, ἢ κατὰ Ἰουδαίων συγγραφόμενον· πολὺν γὰρ ζῆλον εἶχεν εἰς τὸ ἐπιστρέφειν Ἑβραίους ἐπὶ τὴν ἀλήθειαν.

He said to them: "Common to both were the food, the bedding, the clothing, and the abstaining from everything superfluous, 3040and the lowliness, and the poverty, and the self-sufficiency. But abba Theodore the philosopher, barefoot, with a great weakness of the eyes, learned the Old and the New by heart; yet he had as his consolation the company of the brethren and the visits of friends, and much distraction, when he was at work and when he was expounding. Of Zoilus the reader, though, not only his life as a stranger is worthy of praise, but also his solitude, and his measureless toil, and the guard on his mouth, and that he was friendless, owned nothing of his own, kept no company [?], in total idleness from all worldly business; and he gave himself over to no consolation, and would not accept even the barest service from anyone: he cooked for himself, he washed for himself, not even having the refreshment of reading; ready besides to serve others, having no care for cold or heat or bodily sickness; ever clean of laughter and gloom and acedia and dissipation; and besides the roughness of his garment, he was eaten away by a multitude of lice, unremittingly [?]. But this one too, beyond the first, had no small recreation from going out, having full freedom to go out all night and all day; yet the measurelessness of his toil cut short even this freedom, and amid much company he showed himself set apart [?] from men of the world. So each will receive his own reward according to his own toil, and according to the measures of the resurrection, and of purity, the spiritual and that of the mind, and of fear of God, and of love, and of liturgy, and of compunction, and of undistracted psalmody and prayer, and of earnest faith, and of that pleasing of God which is hidden from men and unseen."

CHAPTER 172

About this master Cosmas the scholasticus many told us many things, others other things, and very many very many things. But what we were eyewitnesses of, and followed closely, these we write, for the profit of those who read. For the man was humble, merciful, self-controlled, a virgin, still, without anger, a lover of his companions, hospitable, a lover of the poor. So this wonderful man brought us great profit, not only by being seen and by teaching, but also because he had more books than anyone in Alexandria, and gladly lent them to those who wished. He also owned nothing; in his whole house there was nothing else to be seen but books, and a bed, and a table. Anyone might go in, and ask about the things that profit, and read. Every day I used to go in to him, and, in all truth, I never once went in without finding him either reading or writing against the Jews; for he had great zeal to turn the Hebrews to the truth.

Διὸ καὶ πολλάκις με ἔπεμψεν 3041πρός τινας Ἑβραίους ἵνα ἀπὸ Γραφῆς αὐτοῖς διΑ λεχθῶ, διὰ τὸ αὐτὸν μὴ ταχέως ἐξέρχεσθαι τοῦ οἴκου αὐτοῦ. Τούτῳ τῷ κυρῷ Κοσμᾷ τῷ σχολαστικῷ παρέβαλον ἐν μιᾷ, καὶ ἔχων πολλὴν παῤῥησίαν πρὸς αὐτὸν, λέγω αὐτῷ· Ποίησον ἀγάπην, πόσον χρόνον ἔχεις ἡσυχάζων; Σιωπῶντι δὲ καὶ μηδὲν ἀποκρινομένῳ, πάλιν λέγω αὐτῷ· Διὰ τὸν Κύριον, εἰπόν μοι. Ὁ δὲ μικρὸν ἐπισχὼν λέγει μοι· Ἔχω τριάκοντα τρεῖς χρόνους. Ἐγὼ δὲ ἀκούσας, ἐδόξασα τὸν Θεόν. Ἄλλοτε πάλιν παραβαλὼν αὐτῷ, ἐπερώτησα αὐτὸν λέγων· Ποίησον τελείαν τὴν ἀγάπην, εἰδὼς ὅτι δι᾿ ὠφέλειαν ψυχῆς ἐπερωτῶ σε· λέγε μοι, ἐν τοσούτῳ χρόνῳ τῆς ἡσυχίας καὶ τῆς ἐγκρατείας, τί κατώρθωσας; Ὁ δὲ μέγα στενάξας ἐκ τοῦ βάθους τῆς καρ-δίας δίας, λέγει μοι· Τί ἀνὴρ κοσμικὸς ἔχει κατορθῶσαι καὶ μάλιστα ἐν τῷ οἴκῳ αὐτοῦ καθεζόμενος; Ἐγὼ δὲ πάλιν παρεκάλουν αὐτὸν, λέγων· Διὰ τὸν Κύριον εἰπόν μοι, καὶ ὠφέλησόν με. Τότε πολλὰ βιασθεὶς παρ᾿ ἐμοῦ, λέγει μοι· Συγχώρησόν μοι, τὰ τρία ταῦτα οἶδα, ὅτι κατώρθωσα, τὸ μὴ γελᾶσαι, τὸ μὴ ὁμόσαι, τὸ μὴ ψεύσασθαι.

Ἦν τις ἀναχωρητὴς εἰς τὰ μέρη τοῦ ἁγίου Ἰορδάνου, ὀνόματι Θεόδωρος, εὐνοῦχος. Οὗτος ἠναγκάσθη διὰ χρείαν τινὰ κατελθεῖν ἐν Κωνσταντινουπόλει· καὶ δὴ εἰσῆλθεν ἐν πλοίῳ. Καὶ χρονοτριβησάντος τοῦ πλοίου εἰς τὸ πέλαγος, ἔλειψεν τὸ ὕδωρ, καὶ ἐν πολλῇ ἀθυμίᾳ καὶ ἀμηχανίᾳ ἦσαν οἱ ναῦται καὶ ἐπιβάται. Ἐγερθεὶς δὲ ὁ ἀναχωρητὴς, καὶ ἐκτείνας τὰς χεῖρας εἰς τὸν οὐρανὸν πρὸς τὸν Θεὸν σώζοντα ἐκ θανάτου τὰς ψυχὰς ἡμῶν· καὶ ποιήσας εὐχὴν, καὶ σφραγίσας τὴν θάλασσαν τῷ σημείῳ τοῦ σταυροῦ, λέγει τοῖς ναύταις, Εὐλογητὸς Κύριος· ἀντλήσατε ὅσον δεῖσθε ὕδωρ. Καὶ ἐγέμισαν ὅλα τὰ ἀγγεῖα τοῦ ἐκ τῆς θαλάσσης γλυκέος ὕδατος, καὶ πάντες ἐδόξασαν τὸν Θεόν.

Ὁ ἀββᾶς Γρηγόριος ἀναχωρητὴς διηγήσατο ἡμῖν, λέγων, ὅτι Κατερχόμενος ἐκ τοῦ Βυζαντίου εἰς πλοῖον, εἰσῆλθεν δὲ καὶ εἷς σκριβὼν μετὰ τῆς γυναῖκος αὐτοῦ, ὃς ὥφειλεν εὔξασθαι ἐν τῇ ἁγίᾳ πόλει. Ἦν δὲ ὁ ναύκληρος πάνυ εὐλαβὴς, καὶ νηστευτής. Ὡς οὖν ἐπλέομεν, ἀφειδῶς ἐδαπάνων τὸ ὕδωρ οἱ παῖδες τοῦ σκριβῶνος. Ἐλθόντες δὲ εἰς τὸ μεσοπέλαγος ἔλειψεν ἡμῖν τὸ ὕδωρ, καὶ ἦμεν ἐν πολλῇ ἀνάγκῃ, καὶ ἦν θέαμα ἰδεῖν ἐλεεινὸν, γυναῖκας, καὶ παῖδας, καὶ παιδία μικρὰ τῇ δίψῃ ἐκλελοιπότα, καὶ κειμένους ὡς νεκρούς. Ποιησάντων δὲ ἡμῶν ἐν τῇ τοιαύτῃ ἀνάγκῃ ὡς ἡμέρας γʹ, καὶ λοιπὸν ἐλπίδα ζωῆς μὴ ἐχόντων, ὁ σκριβὼν μὴ φέρων τὴν θλίψιν, ἐξήνεγκεν τὸ ξίφος αὐτοῦ, θέλων ἀποκτεῖναι τόν τε ναύκληρον, καὶ τοὺς ναύτας. Ἔλεγεν γὰρ, ὅτι Οὗτοί εἰσιν αἴτιοι τοῦ ἀπολέσθαι ἡμᾶς, ἐπειδὰν οὐκ ἐγέμισαν εἰς τὴν χρείαν ἡμῶν ὕδωρ.

That is why he often sent me 3041to certain Hebrews, so that I might dispute with them from Scripture, because he himself did not readily go out of his house.

I visited this master Cosmas the scholasticus one day, and since I had great freedom with him, I said to him: "Do me a kindness: how long hast thou been keeping stillness?" When he kept silent and answered nothing, I said to him again: "For the Lord's sake, tell me." He held back a little and said to me: "I have thirty-three years." When I heard it, I glorified God.

Another time I visited him again and questioned him, saying: "Do me the kindness in full, knowing that I ask thee for my soul's profit: tell me, in so long a time of stillness and self-control, what hast thou achieved?" He groaned deeply from the depth of his heart and said to me: "What can a layman achieve, and above all sitting in his own house?" But I besought him again, saying: "For the Lord's sake, tell me, and profit me." Then, hard pressed by me, he said to me: "Forgive me: these three things I know that I have achieved: not to laugh, not to swear, not to lie."

CHAPTER 173

There was an anchorite in the region of the holy Jordan named Theodore, a eunuch. He was obliged by some need to go down to Constantinople, and so he went aboard a ship. And as the ship lingered at sea, the water ran out, and the sailors and passengers were in great despondency and helplessness. The anchorite rose and stretched out his hands to heaven, to God who saves our souls from death; and he made a prayer, and sealed the sea with the sign of the cross, and said to the sailors, "Blessed is the Lord: draw as much water as you need." And they filled all the vessels with the sweet water from the sea, and all glorified God.

CHAPTER 174

Abba Gregory the anchorite told us: "As I was coming down from Byzantium on a ship, there came aboard too a scribo with his wife, who was to pray in the holy city. The shipmaster was very devout, and a faster. As we sailed, the scribo's servants were spending the water without stint. When we came out into the open sea, our water ran out, and we were in great distress; and it was a pitiful sight to see, women and boys and little children fainting with thirst, and lying as if dead. When we had been some three days in such distress, and had no more hope of life, the scribo, not bearing the affliction, drew his sword, meaning to kill both the shipmaster and the sailors. For he said, 'These are to blame for our perishing, since they did not fill water enough for our need.'

Ἐγὼ δὲ τὸν σκριβῶνα παρακαλέσας, 3044ἔλεγον, Μὴ ποιήσῃς τοῦτο, ἀλλὰ μᾶλλον δεηθῶμεν τοῦ Κυρίου ἡμῶν, Ἰησοῦ Χριστοῦ τοῦ ἀληθινοῦ Θεοῦ ἡμῶν, τοῦ ποιοῦντος μεγάλα, καὶ ἀνεξιχνίαστα, ὧν οὐκ ἔστιν ἀριθμός. Ἰδοὺ γὰρ καὶ ὁ ναύκληρος, ὡς ὁρᾷς, τρίτην ταύτην ἡμέραν ἔχει τῇ νηστείᾳ καὶ τῇ εὐχῇ σχολάζων. Καὶ ἡσυχάσαντος τοῦ σκριβῶνος, τῇ τετάρτῃ ἡμέρᾳ ὡς περὶ ὥραν ἕκτην, ἐγερθεὶς ὁ ναύκληρος, μεγάλῃ τῇ φωνῇ ἐκέκραγεν, λέγων· Δόξα σοι, Χριστὲ, ὁ Θεὸς ἡμῶν, ὥστε ἡμᾶς καταπλαγῆναι ἐπὶ τῇ φωνῇ αὐτοῦ. Καὶ λέγει τοῖς ναύταις· Χαλάσατε τὰς διφθέρας, καὶ ὡς ἐφήπλωσεν αὐτὰς, ἰδοὺ νεφέλη ἐλθοῦσα ἐπάνω τοῦ πλοίου, τοσοῦτον ὕδωρ ἔβρεξεν ἕως οὗ ἐγέμισεν πᾶσαν τὴν χρείαν ἡμῶν. Ἦν δὲ τὸ θαῦμα μέγα καὶ φοβερὸν, ὅτι ἀνεμίζοντος τοῦ πλοίου συνηκουλούθει ἡμῖν ἡ νεφέλη, καὶ ἔξωθεν τοῦ πλοίου οὐκ ἔβρεξεν.

Διηγήσατο ἡμῖν τις τῶν Πατέρων περὶ Ζήνωνος τοῦ βασιλέως, λέγων, ὅτι γυναῖκα ἠδίκησεν εἰς τὴν θυγατέρα αὐτῆς. Ἡ δὲ ἐσχόλαζεν εἰς τὸν ναὸν τῆς παναγίας Δεσποίνης ἡμῶν τῆς Θεοτόκου Μαρίας παρακαλοῦσα αὐτὴν, καὶ λέγουσα μετὰ δακρύων· Ἐκδίκησόν με ἀπὸ Ζήνωνος τοῦ βασιλέως. Ὡς οὖν ἐπὶ πολλὰς ἡμέρας τοῦτο ἐποίει, φαίνεται αὐτῇ ἡ παναγία Θεοτόκος, λέγουσα· Πίστευσόν μοι, γύναι, πολλάκις ἤθελον ποιῆσαι τὴν ἐκδίκησίν σου, ἀλλ᾿ ἡ δεξιὰ χεὶρ αὐτοῦ κωλύει με. Ἦν γὰρ ἐλεήμων πάνυ.

Διηγήσατο ἡμῖν ὁ ἀββᾶς Παλλάδιος, ὅτι Ἀκήκοα διηγουμένου τινὸς τῶν Πατέρων, ὀνόματι Ἀνδρέα, ᾧ καὶ ἡμεῖς συνετύχομεν, ὅτι Ὡς ὑπῆρχον νεώτερος, γέγονα ἄτακτος πάνυ. Καὶ πολέμου καὶ ἀταξίας γενομένης, ἐγὼ καὶ ἄλλοι ἐννέα ἐφύγομεν ἐπὶ Παλαιστίνην, ὧν ὁ εἷς μὲν φιλόπονος ἦν, καὶ ὁ ἕτερος Ἑβραῖος. Ἐλθόντων δὲ ἡμῶν ἐπὶ τὴν ἔρημον, ἠσθένησεν ὁ Ἑβραῖος μέχρι θανάτου, καὶ ἐν ἀθυμίᾳ ἦμεν πολλῇ, μὴ εἰδότες τί ποιῆσαι αὐτῷ. Πλὴν οὐκ εἰάσαμεν αὐτὸν, ἀλλ᾿ ἕκαστος ἡμῶν κατὰ τὴν ἑαυτοῦ δύναμιν ἐβάσταζεν αὐτόν. Θέλοντες δὲ ἀγαγεῖν αὐτὸν εἰς πόλιν, ἢ εἰς ἐμπορεῖον, ἵνα μὴ ἐν τῇ ἐρήμῳ ἀποθάνῃ, ἐπειδὰν ὁ νεώτερος, ἐκ τῆς ἀσιτίας καὶ τοῦ σφοδροτάτου πυρετοῦ καὶ τοῦ ἀμέτρου κόπου καὶ τῆς ἐκ τοῦ καύματος δίψης, ὅλως διελύθη, καὶ ἤμελλεν ἐκλείπειν (λοιπὸν γὰρ καὶ πρὸς τὴν ἔξοδον ἦν, καὶ οὐδὲ βασταχθῆναι ἴσχυεν), ἐβουλευσάμεθα μετὰ πολλῶν δακρύων ἐᾶσαι αὐτὸν εἰς τὴν ἔρημον, καὶ ἀπελθεῖν, ὑφορώμενοι τὸν ἐκ τῆς δίψης θάνατον καὶ εἰς ἡμᾶς γενησόμενον. Ὡς οὖν ἐθήκαμεν αὐτὸν εἰς τὴν ἄμμον, κλαίοντες, καὶ εἶδεν ἡμᾶς ἀπιέναι μέλλοντας, ἤρξατο ὁρκοῦν ἡμᾶς λέγων· Τὸν Θεὸν τὸν μέλλοντα κρῖναι ζῶντας καὶ νεκροὺς, μὴ ἐάσητέ με Ἰουδαῖον ἀποθανεῖν, ἀλλ᾿ ὡς Χριστιανόν. Ποιήσατε οὖν ἔλεος μετ᾿ ἐμοῦ, καὶ βαπτίσατέ με, ἵνα κἀγὼ Χριστιανὸς ὑπεξέλθω τοῦ βίου τούτου, καὶ πρὸς Κύριον ἀπέλθω.

But I 3044besought the scribo and said, 'Do not do this, but rather let us pray to our Lord Jesus Christ, our true God, who does great things and unsearchable, of which there is no number. For see, the shipmaster too, as thou seest, has been giving himself to fasting and prayer three days now.' And when the scribo grew quiet, on the fourth day, about the sixth hour, the shipmaster rose and cried out with a loud voice, saying, 'Glory to thee, O Christ our God!', so that we were astounded at his voice. And he said to the sailors, 'Let down the hides.' And as they spread them out, behold, a cloud came over the ship and rained so much water that it filled all our need. And the wonder was great and fearful, for as the ship ran before the wind the cloud kept following us, and outside the ship it did not rain."

CHAPTER 175

One of the fathers told us about the emperor Zeno, that he wronged a woman in the matter of her daughter. And she gave herself up to prayer in the church of our all-holy Lady, the Theotokos Mary, beseeching her, and saying with tears: "Avenge me on the emperor Zeno." When she had done this for many days, the all-holy Theotokos appeared to her, saying: "Believe me, woman, I have often wished to avenge thee, but his right hand stops me." For he was very merciful.

CHAPTER 176

Abba Palladius told us: "I heard one of the fathers named Andrew, whom we too met, telling this: 'When I was younger, I was very disorderly. And when war and disorder came, I and nine others fled to Palestine; and of these one was a philoponos, and another a Hebrew. When we came into the desert, the Hebrew fell sick to the point of death, and we were in great despondency, not knowing what to do for him. Yet we did not leave him, but each of us carried him as far as his strength went. We wanted to bring him to a city or a trading-post, so that he would not die in the desert; but since the young man, from want of food and a most violent fever and measureless toil and the thirst from the heat, was utterly spent, and on the point of failing (for he was now at his departure, and could not even be carried), we resolved with many tears to leave him in the desert and go away, fearing that death by thirst would come on us too. So when we had laid him on the sand, weeping, and he saw that we were about to go away, he began to adjure us, saying, "By the God who is to judge the living and the dead, do not let me die a Jew, but as a Christian. Have mercy on me, and baptize me, so that I too may depart this life a Christian and go to the Lord."

Ἡμεῖς δὲ ἐλέγομεν αὐτῷ Ὄντως, ἀδελφὲ, οὐκ ἔξεστιν ἡμῖν τι τοιοῦτον ποιῆσαι· κοσμικοὶ γάρ ἐσμεν· τοῦτο γὰρ τὸ ἔργον ἐπισκόπων ἐστὶ, 3045καὶ πρεσβυτέρων· πλὴν οὐδὲ ὕδατά εἰσιν ὧδε. Ὁ δὲ ἐπέμεινε τοῖς αὐτοῖς ἡμᾶς ὅρκοις μετὰ δακρύων ἐνορκῶν, καὶ λέγων· Μὴ στερήσητέ με τῆς τοιαύτης δωρεᾶς, Χριστιανοί. Ἐν πλείστῃ οὖν ἀπορίᾳ ὄντων ἡμῶν, θεόθεν ὁ φιλόπονος κινηθεὶς λέγει ἡμῖν· Ἐγείρατε αὐτὸν, καὶ ἐκδύσατε. Καὶ μετὰ πολλοῦ κόπου ὄρθιον αὐτὸν στήσαντες ἐξεδύσαμεν. Ὁ δὲ φιλόπονος πληρώσας ψάμμου τὰς χεῖρας αὐτοῦ, ἐπὶ τρὶς κατέχεεν αὐτοῦ τῇ κεφαλῇ, λέγων, Βαπτίζεται Θεόδωρος εἰς τὸ ὄνομα τοῦ Πατρὸς καὶ τοῦ Υἱοῦ καὶ τοῦ ἁγίου Πνεύματος, ἡμῶν ὑπακουσάντων τὸ Ἀμὴν, καθ᾿ ἕκαστον ὄνομα τῆς ἁγίας καὶ ὁμοουσίου καὶ προσκυνητῆς Τριάδος, καὶ ὡς ἐπὶ τοῦ Κυρίου, ἀδελφοὶ, οὕτως αὐτὸν ἰάσατο, καὶ ἐνίσχυσεν ὁ Χριστὸς ὁ Υἱὸς τοῦ Θεοῦ τοῦ ζῶντος, ὡς μηκέτι φανῆναι ἐν αὐτῷ λείψανον ἀῤῥωστίας· ἀλλ᾿ ὑγιῶς καὶΒ ἐῤῥωμένως ἐν πάσῃ προθυμίᾳ τὸ λοιπὸν τῆς ἐρήμου προέτρεχεν ἡμῶν. Ἐλθόντες δὲ ἐν Ἀσκαλῶνι, ἀνηγάγομεν τὰ περὶ τούτου τῷ μακαρίῳ καὶ ἐν ἁγίοις Διονύσῳ τῷ ταύτης ἐπισκόπῳ, τὸ ὅπως συνέβη τῷ ἀδελφῷ κατὰ τὴν ὁδόν. Ὁ δὲ ὅσιος ὄντως Διονύσιος τούτων ἀκούσας, ἐννεὸς ἐπὶ τῷ παραδόξῳ τούτῳ γέγονε θαύματι, καὶ τὸν κλῆρον ἅπαντα συγκαλεσάμενος, ἀνατίθεται αὐτῷ τὸ πρᾶγμα, καὶ εἰ ἄρα ὤφειλεν αὐτῷ λογισθῆναι εἰς βάπτισμα ἡ τῆς ψάμμου ἐπίχυσις ἢ οὔ. Καὶ οἱ μὲν ἔλεγον ὅτι, ὤφειλεν αὐτῷ λογισθῆναι διὰ τὸ παράδοξον τοῦ θαύματος· οἱ δὲ ἔφασκον, Οὔ. Καὶ γὰρ ὁ θεολόγος Γρηγόριος πάντα ἐξαριθμεῖται τὰ βαπτίσματα, βάπτισμα λέγων Μωσαϊκὸν, ἀλλ᾿ ἐν ὕδατι, καὶ πρὸ τούτου δὲ ἐν νεφέλῃ καὶ θαλάσσῃ βάπτισμα· καὶ τὸ Ἰωάννου οὐκέτι μὲν Ἰουδαϊκόν. Οὐ γὰρ ἐν ὕδατι μόνον, ἀλλὰ καὶ εἰς μετάνοιαν· βαπτίζει καὶ Ἰησοῦς, ἀλλ᾿ ἐν Πνεύματι· τοῦτο ἡ τελειότης. Οἶδα καὶ τέταρτον βάπτισμα τὸ διὰ μαρτυρίου καὶ αἵματος, οἶδα καὶ πέμπτον ἔτι τὸ τῶν δακρύων. Ποῖον οὖν τούτων ἐβαπτίσατο, ἵνα καὶ κυρώσωμεν αὐτό; Καὶ μάλιστα τοῦ Κυρίου πρὸς Νικόδημον λέγοντος, Ἐὰν μήτις γεννηθῇ ἐξ ὕδατος καὶ Πνεύματος, οὐ μὴ εἰσέλθῃ εἰς βασιλείαν τῶν οὐρανῶν· οἷς καὶ ἀντέλεγον ἕτεροι· Τί οὖν; ἐπειδὴ οὐ γέγραπται περὶ τῶν ἀποστόλων, ὅτι ἐβαπτίσθησαν, οὐκ εἰσέρχονται εἰς τὴν βασιλείαν τῶν οὐρανῶν; Πάλιν δὲ τούτοις ἄλλοι ἀντεφέροντο λέγοντες· Ναὶ, ἀληθῶς ἐβαπτίσθησαν, καθὼς Κλήμης ὁ Στρωματεὺς, ἐν τῷ πέμπτῳ τόμῳ τῶν ὑποτυπώσεων μέμνηται· Φησὶ γὰρ, τὸ ἀποστολικὸν ῥητὸν ἐξηγούμενος τὸ λέγον «Εὐχαριστῶ, ὅτι οὐδένα ὑμῶν ἐβάπτισα» ὁ Χριστὸς λέγεται Πέτρον μόνον βεβαπτικέναι, Πέτρος δὲ Ἀνδρέαν, Ἀνδρέας Ἰάκωβον καὶ Ἰωάννην, ἐκεῖνοι δὲ τοὺς λοιπούς. Τούτων δὲ καὶ ἑτέρων πλειόνων λαληθέντων, ἔδοξε τῷ μακαρίῳ Διονυσίῳ τῷ ἐπισκόπῳ ἀποστεῖλαι τὸν ἀδελφὸν εἰς τὸν ἅγιον Ἰορδάνην, κἀκεῖσε βαπτίζεσθαι.

But we said to him, "Truly, brother, it is not lawful for us to do any such thing, for we are laymen; this is the work of bishops 3045and priests; besides, there is no water here." But he persisted, adjuring us with tears by the same oaths, and saying, "Do not deprive me of such a gift, Christians." So when we were at a complete loss, the philoponos, moved by God, said to us, "Raise him up and undress him." And with much toil we stood him upright and undressed him. Then the philoponos filled his hands with sand and poured it three times over his head, saying, "Theodore is baptized in the name of the Father and of the Son and of the Holy Spirit"; and we answered the Amen at each name of the holy, consubstantial and worshipful Trinity. And as before the Lord, brethren, so did Christ the Son of the living God heal him and strengthen him, that no trace of sickness was to be seen in him any more; but sound and strong, with all eagerness, he ran on ahead of us the rest of the way through the desert. When we came to Ascalon, we referred the matter to the blessed Dionysius, now among the saints, its bishop: how it had befallen the brother on the road. When the truly holy Dionysius heard these things, he was struck dumb at this strange wonder; and calling together all the clergy, he laid the matter before them, whether the pouring of the sand ought to be reckoned to him as baptism or not. And some said that it ought to be reckoned to him, because of the strangeness of the wonder; but others said, No. For Gregory the Theologian counts up all the baptisms, speaking of a Mosaic baptism, but in water, and before that a baptism in cloud and sea; and John's, no longer Jewish, for it was not in water only, but also unto repentance; Jesus also baptizes, but in the Spirit: this is the perfection. I know also a fourth baptism, that through martyrdom and blood; I know also a fifth besides, that of tears. "So with which of these was he baptized, that we should even ratify it? Above all, when the Lord says to Nicodemus, Unless one be born of water and the Spirit, he shall not enter into the kingdom of heaven." And others spoke against these: "What then? Since it is not written of the apostles that they were baptized, do they not enter into the kingdom of heaven?" And to these others again replied, saying: "Yes, truly they were baptized, as Clement the Stromatist records in the fifth book of the Hypotyposes. For expounding the apostolic saying that runs, I give thanks that I baptized none of you, he says that Christ is said to have baptized Peter alone, and Peter Andrew, Andrew James and John, and they the rest." When these and many other things had been said, it seemed good to the blessed bishop Dionysius to send the brother to the holy Jordan and have him baptized there.

Τὸν δὲ φιλόπονον ἐκεῖνον διάκονον ἐχειροτόνησε.

3048Ἔλεγεν ὁ ἀββᾶς Ἰωάννης ὁ Κίλιξ, ὅτι Καθημένων ἡμῶν εἰς τὸ Ἔννατον Ἀλεξανδρείας, παρέβαλεν ἡμῖν ὁ Αἰγύπτιος μοναχὸς, καὶ διηγήσατο λέγων, ὅτι ἦλθεν εἰς τὴν λαύραν τῶν κελλίων ἀδελφὸς ἀπὸ ξένης, θέλων καθίσαι· καὶ βαλὼν μετάνοιαν τῷ πρεσβυτέρῳ, παρεκάλει, ἵνα εἰς τὸ κελλίον Εὐαγρίου καθίσῃ. Ὁ δὲ πρεσβύτερος ἔλεγεν αὐτῷ μὴ δύνασθαι καθίσαι ἐκεῖ. Ὁ δὲ ἀδελφὸς ἔλεγεν, ὅτι Ἐὰν μὴ μείνω ἐκεῖ, πάντως ἀναχωρῶ. Λέγει αὐτῷ ὁ πρεσβύτερος· Φύσει τέκνον, χαλεπὸς δαίμων οἰκεῖ ἐκεῖ. Ὁ γὰρ πλανήσας Εὐάγριον, καὶ ἀποστήσας αὐτὸν ἐκ τῆς ὀρθῆς πίστεως, καὶ ἐμβαλὼν αὐτῷ τὰ μυσαρὰ δόγματα, οὐκ ἐᾷ ἐκεῖ καθίσαι τινά. Ὁ δὲ ἀδελφὸς, ἐπέμενεν λέγων, ὅτι Ἐὰν ὧδε μένω, ἐκεῖ ἔχω καθίσαι. Τότε λέγει ὁ πρεσβύτερος· Τῷ κρίματί σου ὕπαγε, κάθου.Καὶδὴ ἀπελθὼν ὁ ἀδελφὸς ἐκάθισεν μίαν ἑβδομάδα, καὶ ἐλθούσης τῆς ἁγίας Κυριακῆς, ἦλθεν εἰς τὴν ἐκκλησίαν. Καὶ ἰδὼν αὐτὸν ὁ πρεσβύτερος, παρεκλήθη. Τῇ δὲ ἐρχομένῃ Κυριακῇ οὐκ ἦλθεν εἰς τὴν ἐκκλησίαν· καὶ παρακαλεῖ δύο ἀδελφοὺς ὁ πρεσβύτερος, ἵνα ἀπελθόντες μάθωσι διατὶ οὐ παρεγένετο εἰς τὴν ἐκκλησίαν· καὶ ἀπελθόντες οἱ ἀδελφοὶ ἐν τῷ κελλίῳ, εὗρον τὸν ἀδελφὸν βαλόντα σχοινίον εἰς τὸν τράχηλον, καὶ ἀπαγξάμενον.

Διηγήσατο ἡμῖν ὁ ἀββᾶς Γεώργιος ὁ πρεσβύτερος τοῦ κοινοβίου τῶν Σχολαρίων, ὅτι εἰς τὰ Μονίδια γέρων μέγας πονικὸς ἐκαθέζετο, ἦν δὲ ἀφελὴς al. ἀμελής. περὶ τὴν πίστιν, καὶ ὅπου ηὕρισκεν, μετελάμβανεν ἀδιακρίτως. Ἐν μιᾷ οὖν ὤφθη αὐτῷ ἄγγελος Θεοῦ, λέγων· Εἰπὲ, γέρων, ἐὰν ἀποθάνῃς, πῶς θέλεις ἵνα ἐνταφιάσωμέν σε; ὡς οἱ Αἰγύπτιοι μοναχοὶ ἐνταφιάζουσιν, ἢ ὡς οἱ Ἱεροσολυμῖται; Ὁ δὲ γέρων ἀπεκρίθη αὐτῷ, λέγων· Οὐκ οἶδα. Τότε λέγει αὐτῷ ὁ ἄγγελος· Σκέψαι, καὶ ἔρχομαι μετὰ τρεῖς ἑβδομάδας, καὶ λέγεις μοι. Ἀπελθὼν οὖν ὁ γέρων πρὸς ἄλλον, διηγήσατο αὐτῷ ἅπερ ἤκουσε παρὰ τοῦ ἀγγέλου. Ὡς οὖν ἤκουσεν ὁ γέρων, ἐννεὸς γέγονεν ἐπὶ τῇ ἀκοῇ· καὶ ἀτενίσας εἰς αὐτὸν ἐπιπολὺ, θεόθεν κινηθεὶς, λέγει αὐτῷ· Ποῦ μεταλαμβάνεις τῶν ἁγίων μυστηρίων; Ὁ δὲ ἀποκριθεὶς λέγει αὐτῷ Ὅπου εὕρω. Τότε λέγει αὐτῷ ὁ γέρων· Μηκέτι δόξῃ σοι ἔξωθεν τῆς ἁγίας καθολικῆς καὶ ἀποστολικῆς Ἐκκλησίας κοινωνῆσαι, ἔνθα ὀνομάζονται αἱ ἅγιαι al. infer. οἰκουμενικαί. τέσσαρες σύνοδοι, ἡ ἐν Νικαίᾳ τῶν τιηʹ, καὶ ἡ ἐν Κωνσταντινουπόλει τῶν ρνʹ, καὶ ἡ ἐν Ἐφέσῳ πρώτη τῶν σʹ, καὶ ἡ ἐν Καλχηδόνι al. Χαλκηδόνι. χλʹ.

And that philoponos he ordained deacon.'"

3048CHAPTER 177

Abba John the Cilician used to say: "When we were living at the Ennaton of Alexandria, the Egyptian monk visited us and told us: 'A brother from abroad came to the laura of the Cells wishing to settle there; and making a prostration to the priest, he begged to settle in the cell of Evagrius. The priest told him he could not settle there. But the brother said, "If I do not stay there, I am certainly going away." The priest said to him, "Truly, child, a cruel demon lives there. For the one who led Evagrius astray, and drew him away from the right faith, and put the abominable doctrines into him, lets no one settle there." But the brother persisted, saying, "If I stay here, it is there that I must settle." Then the priest said, "On thine own judgement be it: go, sit there." So the brother went off and stayed one week; and when holy Sunday came, he came to church. And when the priest saw him, he was comforted. But the following Sunday he did not come to church; and the priest asked two brothers to go off and find out why he had not come to church. And the brothers went off to the cell and found the brother with a rope put round his neck, hanged.'"

CHAPTER 178

Abba George [?], the priest of the coenobium of the Scholarii, told us that at Monidia there lived a great elder, a man of hard labour [?]; but he was simple about the faith, and wherever he happened to be, he communicated without discernment. One day an angel of God appeared to him, saying: "Tell me, elder, if thou diest, how wouldst thou have us bury thee? As the Egyptian monks bury, or as the people of Jerusalem?" The elder answered him, saying: "I do not know." Then the angel said to him: "Think it over; I am coming after three weeks, and thou shalt tell me." So the elder went off to another elder and told him what he had heard from the angel. When that elder heard it, he was struck dumb at the hearing; and gazing at him a long while, moved by God, he said to him: "Where dost thou receive the holy mysteries?" He answered him: "Wherever I happen to find them." Then the elder said to him: "Never again think fit to communicate outside the holy catholic and apostolic Church, where the four holy synods are named: that at Nicaea of the three hundred and eighteen, and that at Constantinople of the hundred and fifty, and the first at Ephesus of the two hundred, and that at Chalcedon of the six hundred and thirty.

Καὶ ὅταν ἔλθῃ ὁ ἄγγελος, λέγε αὐτῷ· Ὡς οἱ Ἱεροσολυμῖται θέλω. 3049Μετὰ οὖν τρεῖς ἑβδομάδας ἦλθεν ὁ ἄγγελος, καὶ λέ-Α γει τῷ γέροντι· Τί ἔνι γέρων; ἐσκέψω; Ὁ δὲ γέρων λέγει αὐτῷ· Ὡς Ἱεροσολυμῖται θέλωΛέγει αὐτῷ ὁ ἄγγελος· Καλῶς, καλῶς. Καὶ εὐθέως παρέδωκεν τὴν ψυχήν. Τοῦτο δὲ ὅλον γέγονεν, ἵνα μὴ τοὺς κόπους αὐτοῦ ἀπολέσῃ ὁ γέρων, καὶ μετὰ αἱρετικῶν κατακριθῇ.

Παρεβάλομεν τῷ ἀββᾷ Ἰωάννῃ τῷ ἀναχωρητῇ τῷ λεγομένῳ Πυῤῥῷ, καὶ διηγήσατο ἡμῖν, λέγων, ὅτι Ἤκουσα λέγοντος τοῦ ἀββᾶ Ἰωάννου τοῦ Μωαβίτου, ὅτι Μονάστριά τις ἦν ἐν τῇ ἁγίᾳ πόλει, πάνυ εὐλαβὴς καὶ κατὰ Θεὸν προκόπτουσα. Φθονήσας οὖν ὁ διάβολος τῇ παρθένῳ, ἐμβάλλει νέῳ τινὶ σατανικὸν εἰς αὐτὴν ἔρωτα. Ἡ δὲ θαυμασία ἐκείνη παρθένος θεωρήσασα al. αἰσθομένη. τῆς τοῦ δαίμονος ἐπιβουλῆς καὶ τῆς τοῦ νέου ἀπωλείας, λαβοῦσα εἰς ἓν μαλάκιον ὀλίγα βρεκτὰ, ἔρχεται εἰς τὴν ἔρημον· καὶ τῷ νέῳ διὰ τῆς ἀναχωρήσεως τὸ ἀνενόχλητον καὶ τὴν σωτηρίαν πραγματευομένη, καὶ ἑαυτῇ τὴν ἐκ τῆς ἐρημίας ἀσφάλειαν. Μετὰ πολὺν οὖν χρόνον, κατ᾿ οἰκονομίαν Θεοῦ, ἵνα μὴ ἄγνωστος ἡ ἐνάρετος αὐτῆς μείνῃ πολιτεία, θεωρεῖ αὐτὴν ἀναχωρητής τις ἐν τῇ ἐρήμῳ τοῦ ἁγίου Ἰορδάνου, καὶ λέγει αὐτῇ· Ἀμμᾶ, τί ποιεῖς εἰς τὴν ἔρημον ταύτην; Ἡ δὲ λαθεῖν θέλουσα τὸν ἀναχωρητὴν, λέγει αὐτῷ· Συγχώρησόν μοι, ὅτι ἐπλανήθην τὴν ὁδόν· ἀλλὰ ποίησον ἀγάπην, Πάτερ, διὰ τὸν Κύριον, καὶ δεῖξόν μοι αὐτήν. Ὁ δὲ θεόθεν γνοὺς τὰ κατ᾿ αὐτὴν, λέγει αὐτῇ· Πίστευσόν μοι, ἀμμᾶ,Σ οὔτε πεπλάνησαι τὴν ὁδὸν, οὔτε ὁδὸν ἐπιζητεῖς· ἀλλ᾿ ὡς γινώσκουσα ὅτι τὸ ψεῦδος ἐκ τοῦ διαβόλου ἐστὶν, εἰπέ μοι τὴν αἰτίαν ἐν ἀληθείᾳ, δι᾿ ἣν ἦλθες ἐνταῦθα. Τότε λέγει αὐτῷ ἡ παρθένος· Συγχώρησόν μοι, ἀββᾶ, νεώτερός τις ἐσκανδαλίσθη εἰς ἐμὲ, καὶ διὰ τοῦτο ἦλθον εἰς τὴν ἔρημον ταύτην, κρίνασα μᾶλλον ὧδε ἀποθανεῖν, ἢ πρόσκομμά τι τινι γενέσθαι, κατὰ τὴν τοῦ Ἀποστόλου φωνήν. Λέγει αὐτῇ πάλιν ὁ γέρων· Καὶ πόσον χρόνον ἔχεις ὧδε; Ἡ δὲ λέγει αὐτῷ· Ἔχω χάριτι Χριστοῦ ἔτη ιζʹ. Πάλιν λέγει αὐτῇ ὁ ἀναχωρητής· Καὶ πόθεν ἐσθίεις; Ἡ δὲ ἐκβαλοῦσα τὸ μαλάκιον καὶ τὰ βρεκτὰ ἐν αὐτῷ, λέγει τῷ ἀναχωρητῇ· Ἴδε τοῦτο τὸ μαλάκιον ὃ βλέπεις, μετ᾿ ἐμοῦ ἐξῆλθεν ἐκ τῆς πόλεως, ἔχον καὶ τὰ μικρὰ ταῦτα βρεκτὰ, καὶ οἰκονομίαν τοιαύτην εἰς ἐμὲ τὴν ταπεινὴν ἐποίησεν ὁ Θεὸς, ὅτι χρόνον τοσοῦτον ἐξ αὐτῶν ἐσθίω, καὶ οὐκ ἠλαττώθησαν. Καὶ τοῦτο δὲ γίνωσκε, Πάτερ, ὅτι οὕτως με ἡ αὐτοῦ ἀγαθότης ἐσκέπασεν, ὅτι τὰ ιζʹ ἔτη ταῦτα, οὐκ ἴδεν με ἄνθρωπος, εἰ μὴ σὺ μόνος σήμερον· ἐγὼ δὲ πάντας ἐθεώρουν.

And when the angel comes, say to him, 'As the people of Jerusalem, I want.'" 3049So after three weeks the angel came and said to the elder: "How is it, elder? Hast thou thought it over?" The elder said to him: "As the people of Jerusalem, I want." The angel said to him: "Good, good." And straightway he gave up his soul. All this happened so that the elder should not lose his toils and be condemned with heretics.

CHAPTER 179

We visited abba John the anchorite who was called Pyrrhus, and he told us: "I heard abba John the Moabite saying: 'There was a nun in the holy city, very devout, and advancing in the ways of God. So the devil, envying the virgin, cast into a certain young man a satanic passion for her. But that wonderful virgin, seeing [?] the demon's plotting and the young man's ruin, took a few soaked pulses in a little bag and went into the desert, procuring for the young man by her withdrawal his freedom from trouble and his salvation, and for herself the safety of the desert. After a long time, by God's dispensation, so that her virtuous way of life should not stay unknown, an anchorite saw her in the desert of the holy Jordan and said to her: "Amma, what art thou doing in this desert?" She, wishing to escape the anchorite's notice, said to him: "Forgive me, for I have lost my way; but do me a kindness, Father, for the Lord's sake, and show it to me." But he, knowing from God how things stood with her, said to her: "Believe me, amma, thou hast neither lost thy way, nor art thou looking for a way; but as one who knows that the lie is from the devil, tell me in truth the reason for which thou camest here." Then the virgin said to him: "Forgive me, abba: a young man was scandalized on my account, and for this I came into this desert, judging it better to die here than to become a stumbling-block to anyone, according to the Apostle's word." The elder said to her again: "And how long hast thou been here?" She said to him: "By the grace of Christ I have seventeen years." Again the anchorite said to her: "And where dost thou get thy food?" She brought out the little bag and the soaked pulses in it and said to the anchorite: "Look at this little bag that thou seest: it came out of the city with me, holding these few soaked pulses; and God has made such a dispensation for me, the lowly one, that for so long a time I have eaten of them, and they have not grown less. And know this too, Father: His goodness has so sheltered me that in these seventeen years no man has seen me, but thou alone today; but I saw everyone."

Καὶ ταῦτα μαθὼν ὁ ἀναχωρητὴς, ἐδόξασεν τὸν Θεόν.

3052Διηγήσατο ἡμῖν ὁ ὁσιώτατος Διονύσιος, ὁ πρεσβύτερος καὶ σκευοφύλαξ τῆς τῶν Ἀσκαλονιτῶν ἁγιωτάτης Ἐκκλησίας, περὶ τοῦ ἀββᾶ Ἰωάννου τοῦ ἀναχωρητοῦ, λέγων, ὅτι Ὄντως μέγας ἐν τῇ παρούσῃ γενεᾷ κατὰ Θεὸν γέγονεν ἄνθρωπος· καὶ πρὸς σύστασιν τῆς αὐτοῦ πρὸς Θεὸν εὐαρεστήσεως, τοῦτο τὸ θαῦμα περὶ αὐτοῦ διηγεῖτο. Ἐν σπηλαίῳ, φησὶν, ὁ γέρων ἐκάθητο εἰς τὰ μέρη Σοχοῦς τοῦ κτήματος ἀπὸ μιλίων μικρῷ πρὸς Ἱεροσολύμων κʹ· εἰκόνα δὲ εἶχεν ὁ γέρων ἐν τῷ σπηλαίῳ αὐτοῦ τῆς παναγίας ἀχράντου Δεσποίνης ἡμῶν Θεοτόκου καὶ Ἀειπαρθένου Μαρίας, ἐν ἀγκάλαις φέρουσαν τὸν Θεὸν ἡμῶν. Καθότι οὖν ἐβούλετο ἀπελθεῖν εἰς τόπον τινὰ, ἢ εἰς ἐρήμους μακρὰς, ἢ εἰς Ἱεροσόλυμα, πρὸς τὸ προσκυνῆσαι τὸν ἅγιον σταυρὸν καὶ σεβαστοὺς τόπους, ἢ καὶ εἰς τὸ Σιναῖον ὄρος, πρὸς τὸ εὔχεσθαι, καὶ εἰς μάρτυρας ἀφεστῶτας Ἱεροσολύμων πολλοῖς καὶ μακροῖς διαστήμασιν (ἦν γὰρ φιλομάρτυς ὁ γέρων, καὶ νῦν μὲν ἀπίη πρὸς τὸν ἅγιον Ἰωάννην εἰς Ἔφεσον, νῦν δὲ εἰς τὸν ἅγιον Θεόδωρον εἰς Εὐχάϊταν al. Εὐχαΐτας., καὶ ἄρτι μὲν εἰς τὴν τῶν Ἰσαύρων εἰς τὴν ἁγίαν al. ὁσιομάρτυρα. Θέκλαν εἰς Σελεύκειαν, ἄρτι δὲ εἰς τὸν ἅγιον Σέργιον εἰς τὸ Σαφᾶς· καὶ ποτὲ μὲν εἰς τοῦτον, ποτὲ δὲ εἰς ἐκεῖνον τὸν ἅγιον ὤδευσεν), ἐσκεύαζεν τὴν κανδήλαν, καὶ ἧπτεν ὡς εἶχεν συνήθειαν, καὶ πρὸς ἱκεσίαν στὰς, καὶ τὴν πορείαν αὐτῷ κατευθυνθῆναι τὸν Θεὸν ἱκετεύσας, ἔλεγεν εἰς τὴν Δέσποιναν, εἰς τὴν εἰκόνα αὐτῆς προσέχων· Ἁγία Δέσποινα Θεοτόκε, ἐπειδὴ ὁδὸν μέλλω βαδίσαι μακρὰν, πολλῶν ἡμερῶν καὶ σβεννυμένην αὐτὴν κατὰ τὴν ἐμὴν πρόθεσιν φύλαξαι· ἐγὼ γὰρ τὴν σὴν βοήθειαν συνοδοιπόρον ἔχων ἀπέρχομαι. Καὶ ταῦτα πρὸς τὴν εἰκόνα φήσας, ἐξήρχετο. Καὶ τὴν προτεθεῖσαν πορείαν ποιήσας, ὑπἐστρεφεν, ποτὲ μὲν διὰ μηνὸς, ποτὲ δὲ διὰ δύων καὶ τριῶν, ἔστιν δὲ ὅτε καὶ διὰ πέντε καὶ ἕξ· καὶ οὕτως εὕρισκεν τὴν κανδήλαν ἐσκευασμένην τε καὶ ἁπτομένην, καθάπερ αὐτὴν πρὸς τὴν ὁδοιπορίαν ἐξερχόμενος εἴμσεν, καὶ οὐδέποτε αὐτὴν ἀφ᾿ ἑαυτῆς σβεσθεῖσαν ἑώρακεν, οὔτε ἐξ ὕπνου ἀνιστάμενος, οὔτε ἐξ ἀποδημίας ἐπαναστρέφων, οὔτε ἐξ ἐρήμου φοιτῶν εἰς τὸ σπήλαιον.

And when the anchorite learned these things, he glorified God.'"

3052CHAPTER 180

The most holy Dionysius, priest and sacristan of the most holy church of the Ascalonites, told us about abba John the anchorite: "Truly he was a great man in God's sight in the present generation." And in proof of how well he pleased God, he told this wonder about him. "The elder," he says, "lived in a cave in the region of Sochous, the estate, a little under twenty miles from Jerusalem; and the elder had in his cave an icon of our all-holy, spotless Lady, the Theotokos and Ever-virgin Mary, carrying our God in her arms. So whenever he wanted to go off to some place, either into the far deserts, or to Jerusalem to venerate the holy Cross and the venerable places, or even to Mount Sinai to pray, or to martyrs lying many long distances away from Jerusalem (for the elder was a lover of the martyrs, and now he would go off to Saint John at Ephesus, now to Saint Theodore at Euchaita, and again to Saint Thecla of the Isaurians at Seleucia, and again to Saint Sergius at Saraphas; and he travelled now to this saint, now to that), he would make the lamp ready and light it, as was his custom; and standing in supplication, and beseeching God to direct his journey for him, he would say to the Lady, fixing his gaze on her icon: 'Holy Lady Theotokos, since I am about to walk a long road of many days, [?] and keep it from going out, as I intend; for I go with thy help as my fellow traveller.' And having said these things to the icon, he would go out. And having made the journey he had set before himself, he would return, sometimes after a month, sometimes after two or three, and at times even after five or six; and so he would find the lamp made ready and burning, just as he had left it when he set out on his journey; and he never once saw it gone out of itself, neither on rising from sleep, nor on coming back from a journey, nor on returning from the desert to the cave."

CHAPTER 181

The same Dionysius the priest also told us this about him: "One day the elder was walking in the bounds of Sochous, the estate, where his cave also was; and as he walked he saw a great lion coming from the opposite way and drawing near.

Ἔλεγεν δὲ ἡμῖν καὶ τοῦτο περὶ τοῦ αὐτοῦ ὁ αὐτὸς Διονύσιος ὁ πρεσβύτερος, ὅτι Ἐν μιᾷ περιεπάτει ὁ γέρων ἐν τοῖς ὁρίοις Σοχοῦς τοῦ κτήματος· ἔνθα ἦν αὐτοῦ καὶ τὸ σπήλαιον· καὶ περιπατῶν μέγαν ἐθέασε λέοντα ἐξ ἐναντίας ἐρχόμενον, καὶ πλησίον γινόμενον. Περιεπάτει δὲ ἐν ὁδῷ πάνυστενῇ μεταξὺ δύο προκειμένῃ φραγμῶν, οἷς οἱ γεωργοὶ περιφράττειν 5 Εὐχαΐτας.ὁσιομάρτυα. 3053αὐτῶν τὰς ἀρούρας εἰώθασιν, τὰ τῶν ξύλων ἀκαν-θώδη θώδη φυτεύοντες· καὶ τοσαύτην ἡ ὁδὸς ἐκ τῶν ἀκάνθων εἶχεν στένωσιν, ὡς μόλις ἕνα τινὰ μηδὲν ἐπιφερόμενον ἐν αὐτῇ πεζεύοντα δέχεσθαι, καὶ ἀκωλύτως μὴ συγχωρεῖσθαι βαδίζειν τὸν ἐν αὐτῇ πορευόμενον. Ὡς δὲ ἀλλήλοις προσήγγισαν, ὅ τε γέρων καὶ ὁ λέων, οὔτε ὁ γέρων ὑπέστρεψεν εἰς τὸ δίοδον δοῦναι τῷ λέοντι, οὔτε διὰ τὸ πάνυ στενὸν τῆς ὁδοῦ ὁ λέων ὑποστρέφειν ἐδύνατο, οὔτε παρελθεῖν ἀμφοτέρους ἐνεδέχετο. Θεωρήσας οὖν ὁ λέων τοῦ Θεοῦ τὸν θεράποντα προβαίνειν βουλόμενον, καὶ μηδαμῶς ὑποστρέφειν θέλοντα, εἰς τοὺς ὄπισθεν πόδας στὰς, καὶ γενόμενος ὄρθιος ἐπὶ τῶν ἐξ ἀριστερῶν τοῦ γέροντος ἀνεκλίθη φραγμῶν, καὶ τῷ βάρει, καὶ τῇ δυνάμει τοῦ σώματος μικρὰν εὐρυχωρίαν ποιήσας ἀνεμπόδιστον τῷ δικαίῳ τὴν πορείαν παρέσχεν· καὶ οὕτω παρῆλθεν ὁ γέρων τὸ ὀπίσθιον προσψαύων τοῦ λέοντος· καὶ τότε μετὰ τὴν αὐτοῦ πάροδον ὁ λέων ἐξαναστὰς τοῦ φραγμοῦ, τὴν οἰκείαν ὁδὸν παρελήλυθεν. Τῷ ἀββᾷ Ἰωάννῃ παρέβαλεν ἀδελφὸς, καὶ οὐδὲν εὗρεν ἐν τῷ σπηλαίῳ αὐτοῦ. Καὶ λέγει αὐτῷ, Πῶς κάθῃ ὧδε, Πάτερ, μὴ ἔχων τὰς χρείας σου; Ἀπεκρίθη ὁ γέρων· Τὸ σπήλαιον τοῦτο, τέκνον, κήρωμά ἐστι· δὸς, λάβε.

Μοναστήριόν ἐστι πλησίον τῆς ἁγίας Βηθλεὲμ, τοῦ ἁγίου Σεργίου, ὡς ἀπὸ σημείων δύο, τὸ ἐπονομάζομενον Ξηροπόταμος. Ἐν τούτῳ ἡγούμενος ἦν εὐλαβὴς πάνυ ὁ ἀββᾶς Εὐγένιος ὀνόματι, ὕστερον δὲ γέγονεν ἐπίσκοπος Ἑρμοπόλεως τῆς κατ᾿ Αἴγυπτον· ἥτις διάκειταιἐν μεθορίοις τῆς πρώτης Θηβαΐδος. Οὗτος διηγήσατο ἡμῖν παραβαλοῦσιν ἐν τῷ μοναστηρίῳ περὶ τοῦ ἀββᾶ Ἀλεξάνδρου τοῦ Κίλικος, ὅτι μετὰ τὸ καταγηρᾶσαι αὐτὸν ἐν τοῖς σπηλαίοις τοῦ ἁγίου Ἰορδάνου, ἔλαβεν αὐτὸν ἐν τῷ μοναστηρίῳ ἑαυτοῦ· καὶ περὶ τὰ τελευταῖα αὐτοῦ, κλινήρης γέγονεν ἐπὶ μῆνας τρεῖς. Καὶ πρὸ δέκα ἡμερῶν τοῦ πρὸς Κύριον ἐκδημῆσαι αὐτὸν, ἐδαιμονίσθη ἐκ φθόνου διαβολικοῦ. Ἤρξατο οὖν ὁ γέρων πρὸς τὸν δαίμονα λέγειν· Πρὸς ἑσπέραν ἦλθες, ἄθλιε. Τοῦτο μέγα οὐκ ἔστιν· ὅτι ἐν κλίνῃ λέλυμαι, καὶ ἀκίνητος ὑπάρχω. Ἔδειξας πάνυ, ἄθλιε, τὴν ἀσθένειάν σου, καὶ μὴ θέλων. Εἰ γὰρ ἧς δυνατὸς καὶ ἴσχυες, ὤφειλες ἐλθεῖν πρὸ ἄλλων πεντήκοντα ἢ ἑξήκοντα ἐτῶν ἐγγύς μου, ἵνα διὰ τοῦ ἐνδυναμοῦντός με Χριστοῦ, ἔδειξά σοι τὴν ἀσθένειάν σου, καὶ κατέβαλόν σου τὸ φρύαγμα, καὶ συνέτριψά σου τὸν ἀκαμπῆ αὐχένα. Νῦν δὲ οὐκ ἐμή ἐστιν ἀσθένεια, ἀλλ᾿ ἡ καταπονοῦσά με. Πλὴν εὐχαριστῶ τῷ Θεῷ, πρὸς ὃν ἀπερχόμενος ἐνδείξομαι τὴν ἀδικίαν ἣν ὑπέστην ὑπὸ σοῦ, ὅτι μετὰ τὴν ἐν τῇ ἀσκήσει τῶν τοσούτων ἐτῶν κακοπάθειαν, περὶ τὴν ἔξοδον ἐπέβης μοι ἀνελεημόνως. Ταῦτα καθ᾿ ἑκάστην ἡμέραν μετὰ καὶ ἄλλων πλειόνων λέγων, τῇ δεκάτῃ ἡμέρᾳ μετὰ πάσης ἡσυχίας τῷ Δεσπότῃ Χριστῷ παρέδωκεν τὸ πνεῦμα αὐτοῦ ἐν εἰρήνῃ.

He was walking along a very narrow path lying between two hedges, with which the farmers are used to fence 3053their fields, planting thorny shrubs; and the path was so narrowed by the thorns that it would barely take one man going on foot and carrying nothing, and would not let the one going along it walk without hindrance. When they drew near each other, the elder and the lion, neither did the elder turn back to give the lion passage, nor could the lion turn back, because the path was so very narrow, nor was there room for both to pass. So the lion, seeing that the servant of God wanted to go forward and would by no means turn back, stood up on its hind feet, and rising upright, leaned back against the hedge on the elder's left; and with the weight and strength of its body it made a little room, and gave the just man unhindered passage; and so the elder passed, brushing against the lion's back. And then, after he had passed, the lion got up from the hedge and went on its own way."

A brother visited abba John and found nothing in his cave. And he said to him: "How dost thou live here, Father, not having thy needs?" The elder answered: "This cave, child, is a wrestling-ground: give, take."

CHAPTER 182

There is a monastery near holy Bethlehem, Saint Sergius's, about two miles off, called Xeropotamus. Its superior was a very devout man named abba Eugenius, who later became bishop of Hermopolis in Egypt, which lies on the borders of the First Thebaid. He told us, when we visited him at the monastery, about abba Alexander the Cilician: that after he had grown old in the caves of the holy Jordan, he took him into his own monastery; and towards his end he became bedridden for three months. And ten days before he departed to the Lord, he was possessed by a demon, out of the devil's envy. So the elder began to say to the demon: "Thou hast come at evening, wretch. This is nothing great, when I lie loosed upon a bed and cannot move. Thou hast fully shown thy weakness, wretch, even against thy will. For if thou wast mighty and strong, thou oughtest to have come near me fifty or sixty years ago, so that, through Christ who strengthens me, I would have shown thee thy weakness, and cast down thy insolence, and crushed thy unbending neck. But now it is not my weakness, but that which wears me down. Yet I give thanks to God, to whom I am departing, and before whom I shall show the wrong I have suffered from thee: that after the hardship of so many years of ascesis, at my departure thou didst set upon me without mercy." Saying these things every day, together with many others, on the tenth day, in all stillness, he gave up his spirit to Christ the Master in peace.

Ἔλεγεν ὁ ἀββᾶς Θεόδωρος ὁ Κίλιξ, ὅτι Καθημένου 3056μου ἐν τῇ Σκήτει, ἦν ἐκεῖ τις γέρων ὀνόματιΔαβίδ. Ἐν μιᾷ οὖν ἀπῆλθεν θερίσαι μετὰ καὶ ἄλλων μοναχῶν. Τοῦτο δὲ ἔθος ἐστὶ ἐπὶ τοῖς Σκητιώταις ἀπέρχεσθαι εἰς τὰ κτήματα, καὶ θερίζειν. Ἀνῆλθεν οὖν ὁ γέρων εἰς κτῆμα, καὶ ἡμερομίσθει. Εἷς δὲ γεωργὸς ἐμισθώσατο αὐτόν. Ὡς οὖν ἐθέριζεν ὁ γέρων, γέγονεν ἐν μιᾷ ἡμέρᾳ περὶ ὥραν ἕκτην καύσων πολὺς, καὶ ἀπῆλθεν ὁ γέρων εἰς μίαν καλύβην, καὶ ἐκάθισεν. Ἐλθὼν δὲ ὁ γεωργὸς, καὶ ἰδὼν αὐτὸν καθήμενον, λέγει αὐτῷ μετ᾿ ὀργῆς· Διότι οὐ θερίζεις, γέρων; Οὐκ οἶδας, ὅτι μισθόν σοι παρέχω; Λέγει αὐτῷ, Ναὶ, ἀλλ᾿ ἐπειδὴ καύσων ἐστὶν πάνυ, καὶ οἱ κόκκοι τοῦ σίτου ἐκ τῶν σταχύων ἐκπίπτουσι, διὰ τοῦτο μικρὸν ἀναμένω, ἵνα παρέλθῃ ὁ καύσων, καὶ μὴ ζημιοῦσαι. Λέγει αὐτῷ ὁ γεωργός· Ἐγείρου, κάμνε, κἂν καῇ ὅλα. Λέγει αὐτῷ ὁ γέρων· Καὶ θέλεις ἵνα καῇ; Λέγει αὐτῷ ὁ γεωργὸς μετὰ θυμοῦ· Ναὶ, εἶπόν σοι. Τότε ἀνέστη ὁ γέρων, καὶ ἰδοὺ ἄφνω ἤρξατο καίεσθαι ὁ ἀγρός. Τότε φοβηθεὶς ὁ γεωργὸς, ἦλθεν εἰς τὸ ἄλλο μέρος, ὅπου οἱ ἄλλοι γέροντες ἐθέριζον, καὶ παρεκάλεσεν αὐτούς, ἵνα ἐλθόντες παρακαλέσωσι τὸν γέροντα, ἵνα εὔξηται καὶ καταπαύσῃ τὸ πῦρ. Ἐλθόντων δὲ αὐτῶν ἔβαλον μετάνοιαν τῷ γέροντι. Ὁ δὲ εἶπεν, Αὐτὸς εἶπεν καῆναι. Ὅμως δὲ παρακληθεὶς ὁ γέρων ὑπ᾿ αὐτῶν, ἀπῆλθεν, καὶ στὰς μεταξὺ τῶν ἤδη καυθέντων, καὶ τῶν μὴ καυθέντων, ηὔξατο, καὶ παραχρῆμα ἐσβέσθη τὸ πῦρ τῆς ἀρούρας, καὶ ἐσώθη τὸ λοιπὸν, καὶ ἐθαύμασαν οἱ πάντες, καὶ ἐδόξασαν τὸν Θεόν.

Ὄντες ἡμεῖς ἐν Ἀλεξανδρείᾳ ἐν τῷ Ἐννάτῳ παρεβάλομεν εἰς τὸ μοναστήριον τοῦ ἀββᾶ Ἰωάννου, τοῦ Εὐνούχου, ὠφελείας χάριν· εὕρομεν δὲ ἄνδρα γεγηρακότα, ποιήσαντα εἰς τὸ μοναστήριον περὶ τὰ ὀγδοήκοντα ἔτη, ἐλεήμονα, οἷον ἄλλον οὐχ ἑωράκαμεν οὐ μόνον πρὸς ἀνθρώπους, ἀλλὰ καὶ πρὸς τὰ ἄλογα ζῷα. Τί γὰρ ἐποίει ὁ γέρων ἄλλο ἔργον οὐκ εἶχεν ἀλλ᾿ ἢ τοῦτο· Ἐγειρόμενος ἀπὸ πρωῒ, παρέβαλλεν τοῖς κυναρίοις ὅλοις τοῖς οὖσιν ἐν τῇ λαύρᾳ, ὁμοίως μὲν ἔβαλλεν σεμίδαλιν τοῖς μικροῖς μυρμηκίοις, σῖτον δὲ τοῖς μεγάλοις, ἔβρεχεν δὲ καὶ παξαμάδας, καὶ εἰς τὰ δώματα ἔῤῥιπτε, ἵνα τὰ πετεινὰ φάγωσιν· οὕτως διάγων οὐκ εἴασεν ἐν τῷ μοναστηρίῳ αὐτοῦ, οὐ θύραν, οὐ θυρίδα, οὐ σπετλὸν, οὐ κανδήλαν, οὐ πίνακα, καὶ ἵνα μὴ ὅλα λέγων μῆκος ἐμποιῶ τῷ διηγήματι, οὐκ εἴασεν ὅλως τῶν ἐπιγείων οὐδὲν, οὐκ ἐκράτησέν ποτε ἕως μιᾶς ὥρας, οὐ λογάρην, οὐ χρῆμα, οὐχ ἱμάτιον, ἀλλὰ πάντα παρεῖχεν τοῖς δεομένοις, πᾶσαν φροντίδα τῶν μελλόντων ποιούμενος. Διηγοῦντο καὶ τοῦτο περὶ αὐτοῦ, τὸ ἐλεῆμον καὶ φιλοσυμπαθὲς παραστῆναι βουλόμενοι, ὅτι Ἐν μιᾷ ἦλθέν τις γεωργὸς πρὸς αὐτὸν, αἰτῶν ἓν νόμισμα ἐκ χρυσοῦ δοῦναι αὐτόν. Ἐπειδὴ δὲ ὁ γέρων οὐκ εἶχεν (οὐ γὰρ ἐκράτει ποτὲ χρυσίον), ἔπεμψεν αὐτὸν, καὶ ἐδανείσατο ἀπὸ μοναστηρίου, καὶ δέδωκεν τῷ ἀνθρώπῳ εἰρηκότι αὐτῷ, ὅτι Μετὰ ἕνα μῆνα παρέχω σοι αὐτό.

CHAPTER 183

Abba Theodore the Cilician used to say: When I was living 3056in Scetis, there was an elder there named David. One day he went off to reap with other monks too. For it is the custom among the Scetiotes to go off to the estates and reap. So the elder went up to an estate and worked for a day's wage. A farmer hired him. As the elder was reaping, one day about the sixth hour there was a great heat, and the elder went off into a hut and sat down. The farmer came, and seeing him sitting, said to him angrily: "Why art thou not reaping, elder? Dost thou not know that I am paying thee a wage?" He said to him: "Yes; but since it is very hot, and the grains of wheat are falling out of the ears, I am waiting a little for the heat to pass, so that thou mayest not suffer loss." The farmer said to him: "Get up, work, even if it all burns." The elder said to him: "And dost thou want it to burn?" The farmer said to him in anger: "Yes, I told thee." Then the elder got up, and behold, suddenly the field began to burn. Then the farmer was afraid, and came to the other part where the other elders were reaping, and besought them to come and beseech the elder to pray and put out the fire. When they came, they made a prostration to the elder. But he said: "He himself said it should burn." Yet, besought by them, the elder went, and standing between what was already burnt and what was not burnt, he prayed; and at once the fire in the field was quenched, and the rest was saved; and they all marvelled and glorified God.

CHAPTER 184

While we were at Alexandria, at the Ennaton, we visited the monastery of abba John the Eunuch, for profit's sake. And we found a man grown old, who had spent some eighty years in the monastery, and merciful as we have never seen another, not only toward men but toward the dumb animals as well. For what did the elder do? He had no other work but this. Rising early in the morning, he would put out food for all the little dogs there were in the laura; likewise he would put out fine flour for the little ants, and grain for the big ones; and he would soak rusks too and throw them up on the roofs, so that the birds might eat. Living so, he left nothing in his monastery: no door, no window, no spetlon, no lamp, no plate; and, not to lengthen the story by telling it all, he left nothing at all of earthly things. He never kept, even for one hour, any cash, any money, any garment, but he gave everything to those in need, taking every care for the things to come.

They told this too about him, wishing to show how merciful he was and how full of fellow-feeling: One day a farmer came to him, asking him to give him one gold piece in gold. Since the elder did not have it (for he never kept gold), he sent and borrowed it from the monastery, and gave it to the man, who had told him, "After one month I will give it back to thee."

Ὡς δὲ παρῆλθον ἔτη δύο, καὶ οὐ δέδωκεν αὐτὸ 3057(οὐδὲ γὰρ ἔσχεν ὁ γεωργὸς), μετακαλεῖται αὐτὸν ὁ ἀββᾶς Ἰωάννης, καὶ λέγει αὐτῷ· Δός μοι τὸ νόμισμα, ἀδελφέ. Ὁ δὲ εἶπεν, Γινώσκει ὁ Θεὸς, ὅτι οὐκ ἔχω. Τότε λέγει αὐτῷ ὁ γέρων· Εὗρόν σοι πόρον, δι᾿ οὗ ὠφέλησας ἀποδοῦναί μοι αὐτό. Ὁ δὲ εἶπεν τῷ γέροντι, Εἴ τι κελεύεις, εἰπέ μοι, καὶ ποιῶ· νομίσας ὁ ἄνθρωπος, ὅτι ἔργον αὐτῷ θέλει ἐπιτάξαι. Τότε λέγει ὁ ἀββᾶς Ἰωάννης· Ὅταν εὐκαιρῇς, καὶ οὐκ ἔχῃς τι ἐργάσασθαι, ἔρχου καὶ βάλε τριάκοντα μετανοίας, καὶ τάξω σοι ἓν κεράτιον, καὶ παρεῖχεν αὐτῷ φαγεῖν καὶ πιεῖν· καὶ συνθέμενος τοῦτο ποιεῖν τῷ ἀνθρώπῳ καθ᾿ ὅτι ηὐκαίρει, ἤρχετο, καὶ βάλλοντι αὐτῷ τὰς μετανοίας, ἐχάρισεν αὐτῷ ὁ γέρων μίαν χάριν, τοῦτό ἐστιν ἓν κεράτιον· καὶ παρεῖχεν αὐτῷ φα γεῖν, καὶ πιεῖν· καὶ ἐδίδου αὐτῷ παξαμάδας εἰς λόγον τοῦ οἴκου αὐτοῦ πέντε. Ὅταν οὖν ἐπλήρωσεν τὰ κδʹ κεράτια, ὅ ἐστιν νόμισμα, ἔλαβεν ἀπ᾿ αὐτοῦ τὸ συντόμιον ὁ γέρων, καὶ ἀπέλυσεν αὐτὸν, δεδωκὼς αὐτῷ καὶ εὐλογίας. Ὁ αὐτὸς ἀββᾶς Ἰωάννης Εὐνοῦχος διηγήσατο ἡμῖν, λέγων, ὅτι Ἀνῆλθον ἐν Θηβαΐδι ἐν τῷ κοινοβίῳ τοῦ ἀββᾶ Ἀπολλὼ, καὶ εἶδον ἐκεῖ ἀδελφὸν νεώτερον ἔχοντα πατέρα κατὰ σάρκα, καὶ αὐτὸν μοναχόν. Συνετάξατο οὖν, ὁ νεώτερος μὴ πιεῖν μήτε ὕδωρ, μήτε εὐκρὰς, μήτε ὕδωρ [ὑγρὸν] ἕως τῆς ζωῆς αὐτοῦ· ἤσθιεν γὰρ σερῆς καὶ τρόζημα καί τινα λάχανα τὰ δυνάμενα παραμυθήσασθαι αὐτῷ ἐκ τῆς δίψης. Εἶχεν δὲ διακονίαν τὸ βάλλειν εἰς τὸν φοῦρνον τοὺς ἄρτους. Ὡς οὖν ἐποίησεν χρόνους τρεῖς, ἠσθένησεν. Ἐν οἷς καὶ πρὸς Κύριον ἀπελήλυθεν. Ἐπειδὴ οὖν ἐκ τοῦ πυρετοῦ ἐφλέγετο, καὶ ἐκ τῆς ἀμέτρου δίψης, παρεκαλεῖτο ὑπὸ πάντων, ἵνα μικρὸν εὔκρατος μεταλάβῃ, καὶ οὐδὲ ὅλως ἠνέσχετο ὁ ἀδελφός. Ὁ ἀββᾶς οὖν τοῦ κοινοβίου μετεκαλέσατο ἰατρὸν, ἵνα τί δύνατόν ἐστιν παραμυθήσηται αὐτὸν τὸν κάμνοντα. Ἐλθὼν οὖν ἰατρὸς, καὶ θεασάμενος τὸν ἀδελφὸν ἐν τοιαύτῃ ἀνάγκῃ, καὶ πολλὰ παρακαλέσας αὐτὸν, εἰς τὸ μικρὸν μεταλαβεῖν εὐκράτου, καὶ μὴ ποιῆσαι αὐτὸν, λέγει τῷ ἀββᾷ· Φέρετέ μοι ὧδε σκάφην μεγάλην, καὶ βαλὼν εἰς αὐτὴν τέσσαρα κιλίκια χλιαροῦ ὕδατος ἐποίησεν βληθῆναι, ἕως τῶν μηρῶν αὐτοῦ ἐπὶ μίαν ὥραν· καὶ διεβεβαιοῦτο ἡμῖν ὁ θεῖος γέρων (ἐκεῖ γὰρ εὑρέθη, φησὶν, ὅτε ἀνήγαγον αὐτὸν ἐκ τοῦ ὕδατος), ὅτι μετρήσας τὸ ὕδωρ ὁ ἰατρὸς παρ᾿ ἓν κιλίκιον εὗρον τὸ ὕδωρ. Ἰδοὺ περὶ ἃ οἱ ἀσκηταὶ ἐπέμενον, ἐγκρατευόμενοι ἄσκησιν, βιαζόμενοι διὰ τὸν Θεὸν ἑαυτοὺς, ἵνα τῶν αἰωνίων ἐπιτύχωσιν ἀγαθῶν. Ὁ αὐτὸς ἡμῖν πάλιν διηγήσατο, ὅτι Ἐν αὐτῷ τῷ κοινοβίῳ εἰσῆλθον εἰς κελλίον ἑνὸς γέροντος, καὶ ἐθεασάμην ὅτι ὅπου ἔβαλλεν μετανοίας, πλὰξ ἦν, καὶ ἐπάνω αὐτῆς ἔβαλλεν, καὶ ὅπου τὰ γόνατα καὶ τὰς χεῖρας ἔπηξεν, πλέον τεσσάρων δακτύλων βάθος ἐκοίλανεν τὴν πλάκα. Τοσαύτας ἔβαλλεν μετανοίας.

When two years had gone by and he had not given it back 3057(for the farmer did not have it), abba John calls him and says to him, "Give me the gold piece, brother." And he said, "God knows that I have not got it." Then the elder says to him, "I have found thee a way by which thou canst [?] give it back to me." And he said to the elder, "Whatever thou biddest, tell me, and I will do it": the man thought he wanted to set him some work. Then abba John says, "When thou hast time and hast nothing to work at, come and make thirty prostrations, and I will reckon thee one keration"; and he would give him food and drink [?]. And having agreed with the man to do this, the man would come whenever he had time; and as he made the prostrations, the elder made him a gift of one favour, that is, one keration; and he would give him food and drink; and he gave him rusks too, five, for the needs of his household. So when he had filled up the twenty-four kerations, which is one gold piece, the elder took his bond from him and let him go, having given him blessings as well.

The same abba John the Eunuch told us: "I went up into the Thebaid, to the coenobium of abba Apollo, and I saw there a young brother who had a father according to the flesh, he too a monk. So the young one resolved to drink neither water, nor warm wine, nor water [any liquid] as long as he lived; for he ate endives [?] and raw greens and some vegetables that could relieve him of his thirst. He had as his service putting the loaves into the oven. When he had done three years, he fell sick, and in that sickness he also departed to the Lord. Now since he was burning with the fever and with his measureless thirst, he was urged by everyone to take a little warm wine, and the brother would not bear it at all. So the abba of the coenobium called a physician, so that, if anything could be done, he might give the sufferer relief. So the physician came; and seeing the brother in such distress, and having urged him much to take a little warm wine, and he would not do it, he says to the abba, 'Bring me here a big tub.' And having put four jars of lukewarm water into it, he had him put in, up to his thighs, for one hour."

And the godly elder assured us (for he was there, he says, when they brought him up out of the water) that the physician measured the water and found it short by one jar. See what the ascetics persevered in [?], keeping self-control in their discipline, doing violence to themselves for God's sake, so that they might attain the eternal good things.

The same told us again: "In that same coenobium I went into the cell of an elder, and I saw that where he used to make his prostrations there was a stone slab, and he made them on it; and where he set his knees and his hands, he had hollowed the slab more than four fingers deep. So many prostrations did he make."

Ἐν Σάμῳ τῇ νήσῳ διηγήσατο ἡμῖν ὁ θεοφιλὴς καὶ φιλόπτωχος Μαρία ἡ μήτηρ τοῦ κυροῦ Παύλου 3060τοῦ κανδιδάτου λέγουσα, ὅτι Ἦν γυνὴ ἐν τῇ πόλει Νισίβει, Χριστιανή· καὶ ὁ ἀνὴρ αὐτῆς Ἕλλην. Εἶχον δὲ νʹ μιλιαρίσια. Ἐν μιᾷ οὖν λέγει ἀνὴρ τῇ γυναικὶ αὐτοῦ, ὅτι Δανείσωμεν τὰ μιλιαρίσια ταῦτα, ἵνα μικρὰν ἔχωμεν ἐξ αὐτῶν παραμυθίαν· ἐπεὶ ἓν καὶ ἓν ἀναλίσκοντες, ὅλα ἔχομεν δαπανῆσαι. Ἀπεκρίθη ἡ γυνὴ, καὶ λέγει αὐτῷ, Ἐὰν κελεύοις δανεῖσαι αὐτὰ, δεῦρο, δάνεισον αὐτὰ τῷ Θεῷ τῶν Χριστιανῶν. Λέγει αὐτῇ ὁ ἀνήρ· Καὶ ποῦ ἐστιν ὁ Θεὸς τῶν Χριστιανῶν, ἵνα δανείσωμεν αὐτῷ; Λέγει αὐτῷ ἐκείνη· Ἐγώ σοι αὐτὸν ὑποδείξω· οὐ μόνον οὐκ ἀπόλλοις αὐτὰ, ἀλλὰ καὶ τόκους σοι παρέχει, καὶ τὰ κεφάλαια διπλασιάζει. Ὁ δὲ λέγει αὐτῇ, Ἄγωμεν, δεῖξόν μοι αὐτὸν, καὶ δανείσωμεν αὐτῷ. Ἡ δὲ λαβοῦσα τὸν ἄνδρα αὐτῆς, ἀποφέρει αὐτὸν ἐν τῇ ἁγιωτάτῃ ἐκκλησίᾳ.Ἔχει δὲ ἡ ἐκκλησίατῆς Νισίβεως πέντε πυλεῶνας μεγάλους. Ὡς οὖν εἰσήνεγκεν αὐτὸν εἰς τὸν πόρτικα τῆς ἐκκλησίας, ἔνθα οἱ μεγάλοι πυλεῶνες εἰσιν, ἔδειξεν αὐτῷ τοὺς πτωχοὺς, εἰποῦσα· Τούτοις ἐὰν παράσχης, ὁ Θεὸς τῶν Χριστιανῶν ταῦτα λαμβάνει. Οὗτοι γὰρ πάντες αὐτοῦ εἰσιν. Ὁ δὲ εὐθὺς μετὰ χαρᾶς παρέχει τὰ νʹ μιλιαρίσια τοῖς πτωχοῖς καὶ ἀπῆλθεν ἐν τῷ οἴκῳ αὐτοῦ. Καὶ μετὰ τρεῖς μῆνας στενωθέντων αὐτῶν εἰς δαπάνησιν, λέγει ὁ ἀνὴρ τῇ γυναικί· Ἀδελφὴ, οὐκ ὀφείλει παρασχεῖν ἡμῖν ὁ Θεὸς τῶν Χριστιανῶν τί ποτε ἐκ τοῦ χρέους ἐκείνου, ὅτι στενούμεθα. Καὶ ἀποκριθεῖσα ἡ γυνὴ, λέγει αὐτῷ· Ναὶ, ὕπαγε ὅπου δέδωκας, καὶ παράσχῃ σοι μετὰ πάσης προθυμίας. Ὁ δὲ δρομαῖος ἀπῆλθεν ἐν τῇ ἁγίᾳ ἐκκλησίᾳ.
