Καὶ γενόμενος ἐν τῷ τόπῳ, ἐν ᾧ τὰ μιλιαρίσια ἔδωκεν τοῖς πτωχοῖς, καὶ γυρεύσας ὅλην τὴν ἐκκλησίαν, ὡς ἐνόμιζεν ἰδεῖν τινα τὸν παρασχεῖν αὐτῷ τι ὀφείλοντα, εἰ μὴ μόνον τοὺς πτωχοὺς καθεζομένους πάλιν· καὶ ὡς διελογίζετο ἐν ἑαυτῷ τίνι εἴποι ἢ τίνα ἀπαιτήσοι, θεωρεῖ ἔμπροσθεν τῶν ποδῶν αὐτοῦ ἐν τῷ μαρμάρῳ ἓν μιλιαρίσιον μέγα κείμενον ἐξ ἐκείνων ὧν καὶ ῥογεύσας ὑπῆρχεν συναδελφοῖς, καὶ κλίνας, καὶ λαβὼν αὐτὸ ἀπῆλθεν εἰς τὸν οἶκον αὐτοῦ· καὶ λέγει τῇ συμβίῳ αὐτοῦ· Ἰδοὺ, ἀπῆλθον εἰς τὴν ἐκκλησίαν ὑμῶν, καὶ πίστευέ μοι, γύναι, οὐκ εἶδον, ὡς εἶπας, τὸν Θεὸν τῶν Χριστιανῶν, καὶ οὐδέν μοι δέδωκεν, πλὴν ὅτι τοῦτο εὗρον τὸ μιλιαρίσιον ἐκεῖ κείμενον ὅπου κἀγὼ δέδωκα νʹ μιλιαρίσια. Τότελέγει αὐτῷ ἡ θαυμασία γυνή· Αὐτός ἐστιν ὁ ἀφανῶς σοι τοῦτο παρασχών. Ἀόρατος γάρ ἐστιν, καὶ ἀοράτῳ δυνάμει καὶ χειρὶ τὸν κόσμον διοικεῖ. Ἀλλ᾿ ὕπαγε, κύριέ μου, ἀγόρασαι ἡμῖν τί ποτε, ἵνα φάγωμεν τὴν σήμερον, καὶ πάλιν αὐτός σοι χορηγεῖ. Ὁ δὲ ἀπελθὼν ἠγόρασεν αὐτοῖς ἄρτον, καὶ οἶνον, καὶ ὀψάριον· καὶ ἐλθὼν δίδωσιν τῇ γυναικί. Ἡ δὲ λαβοῦσα τὸ ὀψάριον, ἤρξατο αὐτὸ καθαίρειν, καὶ ἀναῤῥήξασα αὐτὸ εὑρίσκει εἰς τὰ ἔγκατα αὐτοῦ λίθον πάνυ θαυμαστὸν, ὥστε τὴν γυναῖκα θαυμάσαι εἰς αὐτόν. Οὐ μέντοι ᾔδει τί ἐστιν, πλὴν ὅμως ἐφύλαξεν αὐτόν. Ἐλθόντος δὲ τοῦ ἀνδρὸς αὐτῆς, καὶ ἐσθιόντων αὐτῶν, ἔδειξεν αὐτῷ τὸν λίθον ὃν εὗρεν, λέγουσα· Ἰδοὺ, τοῦτον τὸν λίθον εὗρον ἐν τῷ ὀψαρίῳ.

CHAPTER 185

On the island of Samos the God-loving Maria, a lover of the poor, the mother of the lord Paul 3060the candidatus, told us: "There was a woman in the city of Nisibis, a Christian, and her husband a pagan. They had fifty miliaresia. So one day the husband says to his wife, 'Let us lend these miliaresia out, so that we may have a little comfort from them; for spending them one by one, we are bound to use them all up.' The woman answered and says to him, 'If thou wouldst lend them, come, lend them to the God of the Christians.' The husband says to her, 'And where is the God of the Christians, that we may lend to him?' She says to him, 'I will show him to thee. Not only wilt thou not lose them, but he gives thee interest besides, and doubles the principal.' And he says to her, 'Let us go: show him to me, and let us lend to him.' And she took her husband and brought him to the most holy church. Now the church of Nisibis has five great gateways. So when she had brought him into the portico of the church, where the great gateways are, she showed him the poor, saying, 'If thou give to these, the God of the Christians receives it. For these are all his.' And he at once, with joy, gives the fifty miliaresia to the poor, and went off to his house.

"And after three months, when they were pinched for what to spend, the husband says to his wife, 'Sister, the God of the Christians ought to give us something out of that debt, for we are in want.' And the woman answered and says to him, 'Yes: go where thou gavest it, and he will give to thee with all readiness.' And he went off at a run to the holy church. And coming to the place where he had given the miliaresia to the poor, and going round the whole church, as he thought he would see someone whose part it was to give him something, there were only the poor, sitting there again [?]; and as he was turning over in himself whom he should speak to, or whom he should ask, he sees before his feet, on the marble, one large miliaresion lying, of those which he had given out to the brethren [?]; and he stooped and took it and went off to his house. And he says to his wife, 'Look, I went to your church, and believe me, wife, I did not see the God of the Christians, as thou saidst, and nothing was given me, except that I found this miliaresion lying there, where I too had given the fifty miliaresia.' Then the wonderful woman says to him, 'It is he who gave thee this unseen. For he is invisible, and by invisible power and hand he governs the world. But go, my lord, buy us something, so that we may eat today, and he will supply thee again.' He went off and bought them bread and wine and fish, and came and gives them to the woman. And she took the fish and began to clean it, and slitting it open she finds in its entrails a stone, most wonderful, so that the woman marvelled at it. She did not, however, know what it was, but all the same she kept it. When her husband came, and they were eating, she showed him the stone she had found, saying, 'Look, I found this stone in the fish.'

Ὁ δὲ ἰδὼν, ἐθαύμασεν μὲν καὶ αὐτὸς αὐτοῦ τὸ κάλλος, ἠγνόει δὲ αὐτοῦ τὴν φύσιν. 3061Καὶ μετὰ τὸ φαγεῖν αὐτοὺς, λέγει αὐτῇ· Δός μοι αὐτὸν, καὶ ὑπάγων πωλῶ αὐτὸν, ἐὰν ἆρα καὶ εὕρω λαβεῖν εἰς αὐτὸν τί ποτε. Οὐδὲ γὰρ αὐτὸς, ὡς ἔφην, ᾔδει τί ἐστιν, ἰδιώτης ὤν. Λαβὼν οὖν τὸν λίθον ἀπέρχεται εἰς τὸν τραπεζίτην. Αὐτὸς δὲ ἦν καὶ ἀργυροπράτης. Ἦν δὲ ὁ καιρὸς τοῦ στεῖλαι αὐτόν· (λοιπὸν γὰρ ἑσπέρα ἦν), καὶ λέγει τῷ ἀργυροπράτῃ, Θέλεις ἀγοράσαι τὸ λιθάριον τοῦτο; Ὁ δὲ ἀργυροπράτης ἰδὼν αὐτὸν, λέγει· Καὶ τί θέλεις λαβεῖν εἰς αὐτό; Λέγει αὐτῷ ὁ πωλῶν· Δὸς εἴ τι θέλεις. Λέγει αὐτῷ ἐκεῖνος· Λάβε πέντε μιλιαρίσια. Ὁ δὲ πωλῶν νομίσας αὐτὸν καταπαίζειν, λέγει αὐτῷ· Καὶ παρέχεις αὐτὰ εἰς τοῦτο; Νομίσας δὲ ἀργυροπράτης, ὅτι κατ᾿ εἰρωνείαν αὐτῷ οὕτως ἀπεκρίθη, λέγει αὐτῷ, Καὶ λάβε δέκα μιλιαρίσια. Ὁ δὲ πωλῶν νομίσας πάλιν χλευάζειν, ἐσιώπα. Λέγει αὐτῷ ὁ ἀργυροπράτης· Λάβε σοι κʹ μιλιαρίσια εἰς αὐτόν. Ὁ δὲ ἐσιώπα, μηδὲν ἀποκρινόμενος. Ὡς δὲ εἰς λʹ καὶ μετὰ τοῦτο μέχρι νʹ μιλιαρισίων ἀνῆλθεν ὁ ἀργυροπράτης, ἐξομνύμενος ταῦτα παρασχεῖν ἐν ἀληθείᾳ, τότε ἀπιδὼν εἰς ἑαυτὸν, καὶ εἰς ἔννοιαν λαβὼν, ὅτι εἰ μὴ ἦν τοῦτο πολύτιμον, μιλιαρίσια νʹ οὐ παρεῖχεν εἰς αὐτό· κατ᾿ ὀλίγον δὲ ὁ ἀργυροπράτης ἀναβιβάζων, παρέχει αὐτῷ ἕως τριακοσίων μιλιαρισίων μεγάλων. Καὶ ταῦτα λαβὼν, καὶ τὸν λίθον δεδωκὼς, ἔρχεται πρὸς τὴν γυναῖκα αὐτοῦ χαίρων. Ἡ δὲ ἰδοῦσα αὐτὸν λέγει αὐτῷ· Πόσου αὐτὸ πέπρακας; νομίζουσα ἢ εʹ ἢ ιʹ φόλεων αὐτὸ πεπρακέναι. Ὁ δὲ ἐξενέγκας τὰ τριακόσια μιλιαρίσια, ἔδωκεν αὐτῇ, εἰπὼν τοσούτου πεπρακέναι αὐτό. Ἡ δὲ τοῦ Θεοῦ τοῦ φιλανθρώπου θαυμάσασα ἀγαθότητα λέγει πρὸς αὐτόν· Ἰδοὺ, ἄνερ, ὁ Θεὸς τῶν Χριστιανῶν, ποῖός ἐστιν ἀγαθὸς, καὶ εὐγνώμων, καὶ πλούσιος· ὁρᾷς πῶς οὐ μόνον τὰ νʹ μιλιαρίσια, ἀλλὰ καὶ τὸν τόκον αὐτῶν σοι παρέσχεν δανείσαντι αὐτῷ, ἀλλ᾿ εἰς ὀλίγας ἡμέρας ταῦτα ἐξαπλάσας δέδωκεν. Γίνωσκε οὖν ὅτι οὐκ ἔστιν Θεὸς ἕτερος, οὔτε ἐπὶ τῆς γῆς, οὔτε ἐν τῷ οὐρανῷ, εἰ μὴ αὐτὸς μόνος· καὶ πεισθεὶς διὰ τοῦδε τοῦ θαύματος, πείρᾳ μαθὼν καὶ οὕτως τὴν ἀλήθειαν, γέγονεν εὐθέως Χριστιανὸς, καὶ ἐδόξασεν τὸν Θεὸν καὶ Σωτῆρα ἡμῶν Χριστὸν σὺν τῷ Πατρὶ καὶ τῷ ἁγίῳ Πνεύματι, πλεῖστα εὐχαριστῶν τῇ συνέσει τῆς ἰδίας γυναικὸς δι᾿ ἧς αὐτῷ τὸ γνῶναι Θεὸν ἐν ἀληθείᾳ δεδώρηται.

Παρεβάλομεν εἰς τὸ κοινόβιον τοῦ σπηλαίου τοῦ ἀββᾶ Σάβα, τῷ ἀββᾷ Εὐσταθίῳ τῷ ἡγουμένῳ.Κμερκιάριος ἦν, ὀνόματι Μόσχος. Καὶ διηγήσατο ἡμῖν παραβαλοῦσιν ἐν Τύρῳ· Ὡς ἤμην κομερκιάριος, ἑσπέρας βαθείας ἀπῆλθον λούσασθαι, καὶ εὑρίσκω κατὰ τὴν ὁδὸν γυναῖκα ἱσταμένην εἰς τὴν σκοτίαν. Κἀγὼ ἀπελθὼν προσέβαλον αὐτῇ· ἡ δὲ συνέθετο ἀκολουθεῖν ὀπίσω μου. Ἐγὼ δὲ ἀπὸ τῆς διαβολικῆς χαρᾶς, οὐδὲ ἐλουσάμην· ἀλλ᾿ ὥρμησα εἰς τὸ δεῖπνον;

And he, seeing it, marvelled at its beauty too, but did not know its nature.

3061"And after they had eaten, he says to her, 'Give it me, and I will go and sell it, if perhaps I can get something for it.' For he did not know either, as I said, what it was, being a simple man. So taking the stone he goes off to the banker. He was a silversmith too. It was the hour for him to shut up shop (for it was already evening), and he says to the silversmith, 'Wilt thou buy this little stone?' The silversmith, seeing it, says, 'And what wilt thou take for it?' The seller says to him, 'Give whatever thou wilt.' He says to him, 'Take five miliaresia.' The seller, thinking he was mocking him, says to him, 'And thou wilt give that much for this?' The silversmith, thinking he had answered him so in irony, says to him, 'Then take ten miliaresia.' The seller, thinking again that he was making fun of him, kept silent. The silversmith says to him, 'Take twenty miliaresia for it.' He kept silent, answering nothing. When the silversmith had gone up to thirty, and after that as far as fifty miliaresia, swearing that he would give them in truth, then he looked to himself and took it into his mind that if this were not very precious he would not be offering fifty miliaresia for it. And little by little the silversmith raised the price, and gives him as much as three hundred large miliaresia. And taking these, and handing over the stone, he comes to his wife rejoicing. And she, seeing him, says to him, 'How much didst thou sell it for?', thinking he had sold it for five or ten obols. And he brought out the three hundred miliaresia and gave them to her, saying he had sold it for so much. And she, marvelling at the goodness of God who loves mankind, says to him, 'Look, husband, the God of the Christians: how good he is, and grateful, and rich! Thou seest how he has given thee, who lent to him, not only the fifty miliaresia but their interest too; and more, within a few days he has given them back sixfold. Know then that there is no other God, neither on earth nor in heaven, but he alone.' And persuaded by this wonder, and learning the truth in this way by experience, he at once became a Christian, and glorified our God and Saviour Christ with the Father and the Holy Spirit, giving great thanks for the good sense of his own wife, through whom it had been granted him to know God in truth."

CHAPTER 186

We visited the coenobium of the Cave of abba Sabas, and abba Eustathius the superior. There was a customs officer named Moschus. And he told us, when we visited at Tyre: "When I was customs officer, late one evening I went off to bathe, and on the road I find a woman standing in the dark. And I went up and made advances to her, and she agreed to follow behind me. And I, out of devilish joy, did not even bathe, but hurried off to supper. And I pressed her hard, and she would not bring herself to taste anything.

Καὶ πολλὰ προετρεπόμην αὐτὴν, καὶ οὐκ ἠνέσχετο γεύσασθαι· τέλος πάντων ἀνέστημεν κοιμηθῆναι, καὶ ὡς ἦλθον τοῦ ἀσπάσασθαι αὐτὴν, ἀνέκραξε μεγάλῃ τῇ φωνῇ μετὰ δακρύων· 3064Οὐαί μοι τῇ ἀθλίᾳ!ἔντρομος δὲ γενόμενος ἠρώτων τὴν αἰτίαν· ἡ δὲ πλεῖον κλαίουσα, λέγει, ὅτι Ἀνὴρ μου πραγματευτής ἐστιν, καὶ ἐναυάγησεν, καὶ ἀπώλεσεν ἴδια καὶ ἀλλότρια. Καὶ ἔστιν εἰς φυλακὴν διὰ τὰ ἀλλότρια· καὶ μὴ ἔχουσα τί ποιήσω, ἵνα καὶ ἂν ἄρτον λάβω αὐτῷ, προσεθέμην προδοῦναι τὸ σῶμά μου, ἀπὸ πολλῆς ἀτιμίας, ἵνα εὕρωμεν κἂν ἄρτον. Ὅλα καὶ διήρπασαν. Καὶ λέγω, τί ἄλλο ἐστὶ χρέος; Καὶ λέγει, Ἐπὶ εʹ λίτρας. Καὶ λαβὼν τὸ χρυσίον δέδωκα αὐτῇ, εἰπών· Ἰδοὺ διὰ τὸ κρῖμα τοῦ Θεοῦ οὐχ ἡψάμην σου, δὸς καὶ εὔγαλε, καὶ εὔξασθε ὑπὲρ ἐμοῦ. Εἶτα μετὰ χρόνον τινὰ λοιδορία κατ᾿ ἐμοῦ πρὸς τὸν βασιλέα γίνεται, ὅτι τὰ κομερκίου ἐσκόρπισα· καὶ πέμπει ὁ βασιλεὺς, καὶ διαρπάζει τὴν οἰκίαν μου, καὶ ἀπὸ ὑποκαμίσου ἀνασπᾷ με ἐν Κωνσταντινουπόλει, καὶ δίδωσί με εἰς τὸ δεσμωτήριον, καὶ ποιῶ ἱκανὸν χρόνον μετὰ τοῦ παλαιοῦ καμισίου. Ἡμερούσιον δὲ ἤκουσα, ὅτι ὁ βασιλεὺς βούλεται ἀποκτεῖναί με. Καὶ λοιπὸν ἀπεῖπον τῆς ζωῆς μου, καὶ κλαίοντός μου, καὶ ὀδυρομένου ὕπνωσα. Καὶ θεωρῶ ὡς τὴν ἐλευθέραν τὴν τότε ἔχουσαν τὸν ἄνδρα ἐν τῇ φυλακῇ, καὶ φησι· Τί ἓν τὸ ἔχεις, κύριε Μόσχε; Διὰ τί κατέχῃ ὧδε; Καὶ μοῦ εἰρηκότος, ὅτι Ἐλοιδορήθην, καὶ, νομίζω, ἀποκτενεῖ με βασιλεύς· καὶ λέγει μοι· Θέλεις καὶ λαλῶ περὶ σοῦ βασιλεῖ, καὶ ἀπολύει; Καὶ λέγω αὐτῇ· Καὶ οἶδέν σε ὁ βασιλεύς; Καὶ λέγει, Ναί. Καὶ ἐξυπνίσθην, καὶ διηπόρουν τί τοῦτο. Καὶ πάλιν δεύτερον καὶ τρίτον τὸ αὐτὸ λέγουσα· Μὴ φοβηθῇς· αὔριον γὰρ ἀπολύει. Καὶ περὶ διάφαυσιν λαμβάνουσίν με εἰς τὸ παλάτιον, τοῦτο βασιλέως ἐπιτρέψαντος. Καὶ ὅτε εἰσῆλθον, καὶ εἶδεν μετὰ τοῦ ῥυπαροῦ ἱματίου, λέγει μοι· Σπλαγχνίζομαί σοι, τῷ ἄρτι διόρθωσαι τοῦ λοιποῦ. Ἐθεώρουν δὲ τὴν γυναῖκα ἐκείνην ἱσταμένην δεξιᾷ τοῦ βασιλέως, καὶ λέγουσάν μοι· Θάῤῥει, καὶ μὴ φοβηθῇς. Κελεύει ἀναλαβεῖν με τὴν οὐσίαν μου ὁ βασιλεὺς, καὶ δώσας καὶ ἀγαθὰ πολλὰ μετὰ πολλῆς τιμῆς ἀπεκατέστησέν με εἰς τὸν τόπον μου, ποιήσας με καὶ τοποτηρητήν. Αὐτῇ οὖν τῇ νυκτὶ φαίνεται ἡ αὐτὴ γυνὴ, καὶ λέγει μοι· Οἶδας τίς εἰμι; Μεθ᾿ ἧς ἐποίησας ἔλεος, καὶ οὐχ ἥψω τοῦ σώματός μου, διὰ τὸν Θεόν. Ἰδοὺ κἀγὼ ᾕρησάμην σε ἀπὸ κινδύνου. Βλέπεις τὴν φιλανθρωπίαν τοῦ Θεοῦ; τοῦτ᾿ ἔστιν, δι᾿ ἐμὲ ἐποίησας, κἀγὼ ἐμεγάλυνα ἔλεός μου ἐπὶ σέ.

Ἀπερχομένων ἡμῶν ἀπὸ τῆς ἁγίας Γεθσημανῆς ἐν τῷ ἁγίῳ ὄρει τῶν Ἐλαιῶν, μοναστήριόν ἐστι τοῦ ἀββᾶ Ἀβραμίου ἐπονομαζόμενον, ὃ καὶ συνεστήσατο ὁ μέγας Ἀβράμιος τῆς νέας ἐκκλησίας τῆς πανυμνήτου Θεοτόκου καὶ Ἀειπαρθένου Μαρίας, γενόμενος μετὰ Εὐδόξιον ἡγούμενος.

At last we got up to go to bed, and as I came to embrace her, she cried out with a loud voice, with tears,

3064"'Woe is me, wretched woman!' And I, trembling, asked her the cause. And she, weeping the more, says, 'My husband is a merchant, and he was shipwrecked and lost his own goods and other people's. And he is in prison for the other people's; and not having what to do, so that I might at least get him bread, I resolved to sell my body, out of great want, so that we might find at least bread. They have even seized everything.' And I say, 'And what is the debt?' [?] And she says, 'About five pounds.' And I took the gold and gave it to her, saying, 'See, for fear of the judgement of God I have not touched thee. Give it, and get him out, and pray for me, both of you.'

"Then after some time a slander is laid against me before the emperor, that I had squandered the customs' money; and the emperor sends and plunders my house, and drags me off in my shirt to Constantinople, and puts me in prison, and I spend a long time there in my old shirt. One day [?] I heard that the emperor meant to kill me. And so I gave up hope of my life; and as I wept and lamented, I fell asleep. And I see, as it were, the lady who had then had her husband in prison, and she says, 'What is the matter with thee, lord Moschus? Why art thou held here?' And when I had said, 'I have been slandered, and I think the emperor will kill me,' she says to me, 'Wilt thou have me speak to the emperor about thee, and he releases thee?' And I say to her, 'And does the emperor know thee?' And she says, 'Yes.' And I woke, and was at a loss what this was. And again, a second and a third time, she was saying the same: 'Fear not; for tomorrow he releases thee.'

"And about daybreak they take me to the palace, at the emperor's order. And when I went in, and he saw me in my filthy garment, he says to me, 'I take pity on thee for now; mend thy ways from here on.' And I saw that woman standing at the emperor's right hand and saying to me, 'Take courage, and fear not.' The emperor orders that I take back my property; and giving me many good things besides, with much honour he restored me to my place, making me his deputy as well.

"That same night the same woman appears and says to me, 'Dost thou know who I am? She to whom thou didst show mercy, and thou didst not touch my body, for God's sake. See, I too have delivered thee from danger. Dost thou see the loving-kindness of God? That is: Thou didst it for my sake, and I have magnified my mercy upon thee.'"

CHAPTER 187

As we were going from holy Gethsemane to the holy Mount of Olives, there is a monastery called that of abba Abramius, which the great Abramius founded, he who was of the New Church of the all-praised Theotokos and Ever-Virgin Mary, having become its superior after Eudoxius.

Ἐν τούτῳ τῷ μοναστηρίῳ ὁ ἡγούμενος ἦν ὁ ἀββᾶς Ἰωάννης ὁ Κυζηκηνός. 3065Ἠρωτήσαμεν οὖν αὐτὸν ἐν μιᾷ λέγοντες, ΠῶςΑ κτᾶταί τις ἀρετάς; Καὶ ἀπεκρίθη ὁ γέρων· Ἐὰν θέλει τις ἀρετὴν κτήσασθαι, εἰ μὴ πρῶτον μισήσῃ τὴν ἀντιδιάμετρον αὐτῆς κακίαν, οὐ δύναται κτήσασθαι αὐτήν. Ἐὰν οὖν θέλῃς τὸ πένθος, μίσησον τὸν γέλωτα. Θέλεις ταπείνωσιν ἔχειν; μίσησον τὴν ὑπερηφανίαν. Θέλεις ἐγκρατὴς εἶναι; μίσησον τὴν λαιμαργίαν. Θέλεις σώφρων εἶναι; μίσησον τὴν λαγνείαν. Θέλεις ἀκτήμων εἶναι; μίσησον τὴν ὕλην. Θέλεις ἐλεήμων εἶναι; μίσησον τὴν φιλαργυρίαν. Ὁ θέλων οἰκεῖν τὴν ἔρημον, μισεῖ τὰς πόλεις· ὁ θέλων ἡσυχίαν ἀσκεῖν, μισεῖ τὴν παῤῥησίαν· ὁ θέλων ξένος εἶναι, μισεῖ τὴν φανητίαν· ὁ θέλων ἐγκρατὴς γενέσθαι τῆς ὀργῆς, μισεῖ τὸ μετὰ κοσμικῶν διάγειν· ὁ δὲ θέλων ἀμνησίκακος εἶναι, μισεῖ τὰς λοιδορίας· ὁ θέλων ἀπερίσπαστος εἶναι,Β καταμόνας μενεῖ· ὁ θέλων κρατῆσαι γλώσσης, φραξάτω τὰ ὦτα αὐτοῦ τοῦ μὴ ἀκούειν πολλά· ὁ θέλων τὸν φόβον τοῦ Θεοῦ ἔχειν ἀεὶ, μισήσει τὴν σωματικὴν ἀνάπαυσιν, ἀγαπήσει δὲ τὴν θλίψιν καὶ τὴν στενοχωρίαν· καὶ οὕτως δυνήσεται δουλεῦσαι εἰλικρινῶς τῷ Θεῷ.

Ὁ ἀββᾶς Θεόδωρος ὁ ἡγούμενος τῆς παλαιᾶς λαύρας, διηγήσατο ἡμῖν, ὅτι Δύο ἦσαν ἀδελφοὶ ἐν Κωνσταντινουπόλει ἀργυροπράται Σύροι. Καὶ λέγει ὁ μειζότερος ἀδελφὸς τῷ μικροτέρῳ· Δεῦρο, κατέλθωμεν εἰς Συρίαν, καὶ κτήσωμεν τὸν γονικὸν ἡμῶν οἶκον. Λέγει αὐτῷ ὁ μικρότερος· Καὶ διατὶ οἱ δύο, ἵνα ἐάσωμεν τὸ ἀργυροπράτιον; Ἀλλ᾿ ἢ κάτελθε, καὶ μενῶ ὧδε, ἢ κατέρχομαι, καὶ μεῖνον αὐτός. Δοκεῖ οὖν ἀπὸ συμβάσεως, ἵνα κατέλθῃ ὁ μικρός. Ὡς οὖν κατῆλθεν ὁ μικρότερος, μετ᾿ ὀλίγον χρόνον θεωρεῖ καθ᾿ ὕπνους ὁ ἐν Κωνσταντινουπόλει μείνας, εἶδε γέροντα ἱεροπρεπῆ, λέγοντα αὐτῷ· Οἶδας ὅτι ἀδελφός σου ἐπόρνευσεν εἰς τὴν γυναῖκα τοῦ καπήλου; Ὁ δὲ ἐγερθεὶς ἐλυπήθη, λέγων ἐν ἑαυτῷ· Ἐγώ εἰμι ὁ αἴτιος· διατὶ γὰρ ἀπέλυσα αὐτὸν μόνον; Καὶ πάλιν μετά τινα χρόνον θεωρεῖ τὸν αὐτὸν λέγοντα αὐτῷ· Οἶδας ὅτι ὁ ἀδελφός σου ἔπεσεν εἰς τὴν γυναῖκα τοῦ καπήλου; Πάλιν ἰδὼν αὐτὸν ἐλυπήθη. Πάλιν τρίτον μετὰ χρόνον θεωρεῖ τὸν αὐτὸν λέγοντα αὐτῷ· Οὐκ οἶδας ὅτι ὁ ἀδελφός σου ἀπώλεσεν τὴν ἐλευθερίαν, καὶ εἰς τὴν τοῦ καπήλου ἐξεκυλίσθη; Καὶ γράφει αὐτῷ ἀπὸ Κωνσταντινουπόλεως εἰς Συρίαν, ὅτι Εὐθὺς καὶ παραχρῆμα εἴασον ἅπαντα, καὶ κατάλαβε τὸ Βυζάντιον. Ὁ δὲ δεξάμενος τὰ γράμματα, ἐάσας πάντα, ἀνῆλθεν πρὸς τὸν ἀδελφὸν αὐτοῦ. Τότε ὡς εἶδεν αὐτὸν, λαμβάνει αὐτὸν εἰς τὴν Μεγάλην ἐκκλησίαν, καὶ ἤρξατο μετὰ λύπης ἐγκαλεῖν αὐτὸν, λέγων· Καλῶς, ὅτι ἐπόρνευσας εἰς τὴν γυναῖκα τοῦ καπήλου; Ὁ δὲ ἀκούσας ἤρξατο ὀμνύειν τοῦ Θεοῦ τοῦ παντοκράτορος, ὅτι Οὐκ οἶδα τί λέγεις, οὔτε ἐπόρνευσα οὔτε οἶδα εἰς ἁμαρτίαν, εἰ μὴ τὴν νομικὴν γυναῖκα. Ὡς οὖν ἤκουσεν ὁ ἀδελφὸς ὁ μείζων ταῦτα, λέγει αὐτῷ· Οὐκοῦν ἄλλο τί σοι χαλεπώτερον πέπρακται;

In this monastery the superior was abba John 3065of Cyzicus. So we asked him one day, saying, "How does one acquire virtues?" And the elder answered: "If someone wants to acquire a virtue, unless he first hates the vice diametrically opposed to it, he cannot acquire it. So if thou wouldst have mourning, hate laughter. Wouldst thou have lowliness? Hate pride. Wouldst thou be self-controlled? Hate gluttony. Wouldst thou be chaste? Hate lust. Wouldst thou be without possessions? Hate matter. Wouldst thou be merciful? Hate the love of money. He who wants to live in the desert hates the cities; he who wants to practise stillness hates familiarity; he who wants to be a stranger hates making a show of himself; he who wants to become master of his anger hates living among laymen; and he who wants to be free of rancour hates abuse; he who wants to be undistracted will stay alone; he who wants to master his tongue, let him stop his ears so as not to hear much; he who wants always to have the fear of God will hate bodily rest and will love affliction and straitness; and so he will be able to serve God sincerely."

CHAPTER 188

Abba Theodore, the superior of the Old Laura, told us: "There were two brothers in Constantinople, silversmiths, Syrians. And the elder brother says to the younger, 'Come, let us go down to Syria and take possession of our family house.' The younger says to him, 'And why both of us, so that we leave the silversmith's shop? Either thou go down and I will stay here, or I go down and thou stay.' So it is settled by agreement that the younger should go down. So when the younger had gone down, after a little time the one who had stayed in Constantinople sees in his sleep: he saw an elder of priestly bearing, saying to him, 'Dost thou know that thy brother has committed fornication with the tavern-keeper's wife?' And he woke and was grieved, saying in himself, 'I am the cause; for why did I let him go alone?' And again after some time he sees the same one saying to him, 'Dost thou know that thy brother has fallen with the tavern-keeper's wife?' Again, having seen him, he was grieved. Again, a third time, after a while, he sees the same one saying to him, 'Dost thou not know that thy brother has lost the free woman, and has rolled away to the tavern-keeper's?' And he writes to him from Constantinople to Syria: 'At once, this very moment, leave everything and come to Byzantium.' And he received the letter, left everything, and went up to his brother. Then, when he saw him, he takes him to the Great Church, and began with grief to reproach him, saying, 'Well done, that thou hast committed fornication with the tavern-keeper's wife?' And when he heard this he began to swear by God the Almighty, 'I do not know what thou art saying: I have neither committed fornication, nor have I known any woman in sin, except my lawful wife.' So when the elder brother heard this, he says to him, 'Then hast thou done something else, worse?' And he affirmed this: 'I know of nothing out of the way that I have done, except that I found monks in our village of the doctrine of Severus, and, not knowing whether it is wrong, I communicated with them.

Ὁ δὲ διέβαλεν τοῦτο· Οὐδὲν οἶδα ἄτοπον διαπραξάμενος, εἰ μὴ ὅτι μοναχοὺς εὗρον εἰς τὴν κώμην ἡμῶν τοῦ δόγματος Σεβήρου, καὶ ἀγνοῶν εἰ κακόν ἐστιν, ἐκοινώνουν αὐτοῖς· ἄλλο τιποτοῦν 3068οὐκ οἶδα, ὅ τι ἐποίησα.Εότε συνῆκεν ὁ μείζων ἀδελφὸς, ὅτι τοῦτο ἦν τὸ πορνεῦσαιαὐτὸν ὅτι τὴν ἁγίαν καθολικὴν Ἐκκλησίαν εἴασεν, εἰς τὴν αἵρεσιν Σεβήρου τοῦ Ἀκεφάλου τοῦ ὄντος καπήλου, ἔπεσεν, καὶ κατῃσχύνθη, καὶ τὴν εὐγένειαν τῆς ὀρθῆς κατεμόλυνεν πίστεως.

Παρεβάλομεν ἐν Ἀσκάλωνι, ἐν τῷ ξενοδοχείῳ τῶν Πατέρων· καὶ διηγήσατο ἡμῖν ὁ ἀββᾶς Εὐσέβιος ὁ πρεσβύτερος, ὅτι πραγματευτής τις τῆς πόλεως ἡμῶν πλεύσας, ἀπώλεσε τὰ ἴδια καὶ τὰ ἀλλότρια, ἐσώθη δὲ αὐτὸς ὁ μόνος. Ἐλθὼν οὖν ἐνταῦθα καὶ κρατηθεὶς ὑπὸ τῶν δανεισάντων αὐτῷ, ἐβλήθη εἰς φυλακὴν, διαρπαγεὶς καὶ πάντα τὰ ἐν τῷ οἴκῳ αὐτοῦ. Καὶ οὐ κατελείφθη αὐτῷ πλὴν ὧν ἐφόρει αὐτὸς, καὶ ἡ αὐτοῦ γαμετή. Ἐκείνη οὖν ἀπὸ πολλῆς ἀδημονίας καὶ στενώσεως ὡς ἐντολὴν ἐλάμβανεν, ἵνα κἂν ἄρτῳ θρέψῃ τὸν ἄνδρα. Καθημένης οὖν αὐτῆς ἐν μιᾷ καὶ ἐσθιούσης μετὰ τοῦ ἀνδρὸς αὐτῆς εἰς τὴν φυλακὴν, εἰσῆλθε μέγα πρόσωπον δοῦναι εὐποιίαν τοῖς ἐμβεβλημένοις ἐκεῖσε, καὶ ἑωρακὼς τὴν ἐλευθέραν ἅμα τῷ ἀνδρὶ αὐτῆς καθεζομένην, ἐτρώθη εἰς αὐτὴν (ἦν γὰρ ὡραιοτάτη πάνυ), καὶ δηλοῖ αὐτὴν διὰ φυλακίτου. Ἡ δὲ ἀσμένως ἀπῆλθεν προσδοκῶσα ἀγάπην λαβεῖν. Καὶ λαβὼν αὐτὴν κατ᾿ ἰδίαν λέγει αὐτῇ· Τί ἐστιν ὃ ἔχεις; διὰ τί εἶ ὧδε; Καὶ διηγήσατο αὐτῷ τὸ πᾶν. Καὶ λέγει αὐτῇ· Ἐὰν πληρῶ τὸ χρέος σου, κοιμᾶσαι μετ᾿ ἐμοῦ τὴν νύκτα ταύτην; Ἡ δὲ ὡς ἀληθῶς ὠραιοτάτη καὶ σώφρων, λέγει αὐτῷ· Ἤκουσα, δέσποτά μου, τοῦ Ἀποστόλου λέγοντος, ὅτι ἡ γυνὴ οὐκ ἔχει ἐξουσίαν τοῦ ἰδίου σώματος, ἀλλ᾿ ὁ ἀνήρ. Εἴασον, δέσποτα, ἐρωτήσω τὸν ἄνδρα μου· καὶ ὃ κελεύει, ποιῶ. Καὶ ἐλθοῦσα ἀνήγγειλεν τῷ ἀνδρὶ πάντα. Ὁ δὲ γέμων τῆς συνέσεως καὶ στοργῆς πρὸς τὴν ἑαυτοῦ γυναῖκα, οὐ συνηρπάγη τοῦ ἐλευθερωθῆναι τῆς φρουρᾶς· ἀλλὰ μέγα στενάξας, μετὰ δακρύων λέγει τῇ γυναικὶ αὐτοῦ· Ἄπελθε καὶ ἀποσύνταξαι, ἀδελφὴ, τῷ ἀνθρώπῳ, καὶ ἐλπίζομεν εἰς τὸν Θεὸν, ὅτι οὐ μὴ ἐγκαταλίπῃ ἡμᾶς εἰς τέλος. Καὶ ἀναστᾶσα, ἀπέλυσεν τὸν ἄνθρωπον, εἰποῦσα· Εἶπον τῷ ἀνδρί μου, καὶ οὐκ ἠθέλησεν. Καὶ κατ᾿ ἐκεῖνον δὲ τὸν καιρὸν, ἦν τις ὁδοστάτης ἐμβεβλημένος τῇ φυλακῇ ἐνδότερον, καὶ πάντα κατεσκόπει τοῦ ἀνδρὸς καὶ τῆς γυναικὸς διαλεγομένων, καὶ ἐστέναξεν καθ᾿ ἑαυτὸν, λέγων· Ἰδοὺ ἐν ποίᾳ περιστάσει οὗτοι τυγχάνουσι, καὶ τὴν ἐλευθερίαν ἑαυτῶν οὐ προδέδωκαν διὰ χρήματα τοῦ λαβεῖν καὶ τοῦ ἐλευθερωθῆναι. Ἀλλὰ τὴν σωφροσύνην πλούτου παντὸς ἡγήσαντο, καὶ τῶν ἐν τῷ βίῳ τούτῳ πάντων κατεφρόνησαν· καὶ τί ποιήσω ἐγὼ ὁ ἄθλιος, ὁ μηδέποτε ἐν νῷ λαβὼν, ὅτι κἂν ἔστι Θεός· καὶ διὰ τοῦτο καὶ φόνων πολλῶν εἰμι αἴτιος; Καὶ καλέσας αὐτοὺς πρὸς ἑαυτὸν διὰ τῆς θυρίδος, οὗ ἦν ἐμβεβλημένος, λέγει αὐτοῖς· Ἐγὼ λῃστὴς ἤμην, καὶ πολλὰ κακὰ, καὶ πολλοὺς φόνους ἐποίησα, καὶ οἶδα ὅτι ἣν ἂν ὥραν ἔλθῃ ὁ ἡγεμὼν, καὶ ἔλθω εἰς πρόοδον, ἀποθανεῖν ἔχω ὡς φονεύς.

Anything else whatever, 3068I do not know that I have done.' Then the elder brother understood that this was his fornication: that he had left the holy catholic Church and fallen into the heresy of Severus the Acephalite, who is the tavern-keeper, and had been put to shame, and had defiled the nobility of the right faith."

CHAPTER 189

We visited Ascalon, at the guest-house of the Fathers; and abba Eusebius the priest told us: "A merchant of our city sailed and lost his own goods and other people's, but he himself alone was saved. So when he came here and was seized by those who had lent to him, he was cast into prison, and everything in his house was plundered too. And nothing was left him except what he himself had on, and his wife. So she, out of great distress and straitness, took it as her charge [?] to feed her husband, even if only with bread. So as she was sitting one day and eating with her husband in the prison, a great personage came in to give a charity to those cast in there; and seeing the lady sitting with her husband, he was smitten with her (for she was very beautiful indeed), and sends word to her by the jailer. And she went gladly, expecting to receive some charity. And taking her aside he says to her, 'What is the matter with thee? Why art thou here?' And she told him everything. And he says to her, 'If I pay thy debt, wilt thou sleep with me this night?' And she, who was truly most beautiful and chaste, says to him, 'I have heard the Apostle, my master, saying that the wife has not authority over her own body, but the husband. Allow me, master: I will ask my husband, and what he bids, I will do.' And she came and told her husband everything. And he, full of good sense and of love for his own wife, was not carried away by the thought of being freed from the gaol; but groaning deeply, he says with tears to his wife, 'Go and send the man away, sister; and we hope in God that he will not forsake us to the end.' And she rose and dismissed the man, saying, 'I told my husband, and he would not.'

"And at that very time there was a highwayman cast into the prison further in, and he was watching everything as the husband and wife talked together; and he groaned within himself, saying, 'See in what a plight these are, and they have not betrayed their freedom for money, to take it and be freed. But they have counted chastity above all wealth, and have despised all the things of this life. And what shall I do, wretch that I am, who never once took it into my mind that there is a God at all, and for this reason am the cause of many murders too?' And calling them to himself through the window where he was shut in, he says to them, 'I was a robber, and I did many evil things and many murders, and I know that whatever hour the governor comes and I come out to trial, I am to die as a murderer.

Ἰδὼν οὖν τὴν σωφροσύνην 3069ὑμῶν, κατενύγην εἰς ὑμᾶς· ἀλλ᾿ ἀπέλθατε εἰς τόνδε τὸν τόπον τοῦ τείχους τῆς πόλεως, καὶ ὠρύξαντες λάβετε τὰ χρήματα ἃ εὑρήσετε ἐκεῖ. Καὶ ἔχετε αὐτὰ, ἵνα πληρώσητε τὰ δάνεια ὑμῶν, καὶ ἄλλην πολλὴν εὐλογίαν. Καὶ εὔξασθέ μοι, ἵνα κἀγὼ εὕρω ἔλεος. Καὶ μετ᾿ ὀλίγας ἡμέρας ἐπιστὰς τῇ πόλει ὁ ἡγεμῶν ἐκέλευσεν τὸν λῃστὴν ἐξενεχθῆναι, καὶ ἀποκεφαλισθῆναι· καὶ μετὰ μίαν ἡμέραν, λέγει ἡ γυνὴ τῷ ἀνδρὶ αὐτῆς· Κελεύεις, κύριέ μου, ἀπέρχομαι ὅπου ἡρμήνευσεν ἡμῖν ὁ λῃστὴς, καὶ βλέπω εἰ ἄρα ἡλήθευσεν; Ὁ δὲ εἶπεν· Ὡς δοκεῖ σοι, ποίησον. Ἡ δὲ λαβοῦσα μικρὸν σκαλίδιον, ἑσπέρας, καὶ ἐπιστᾶσα τῷ τόπῳ, καὶ ὀρύξασα, εὗρεν χύτραν ἐσκεπασμένην, ἔχουσαν τὸ χρυσίον. Καὶ φρονίμως χρησαμένη, κατὰ μικρὸν δέδωκεν· ὡς δῆθεν ἀπὸ τούτου καὶ τοῦ ἄλλου δανειζομένη, ἕως πάντας ἐπλήρωσεν· καὶ μετὰ ταῦτα ἐξέβαλε καὶ τὸν ἄνδρα αὐτῆς. Ἔλεγε δὲ ὁ διηγησάμενος ἡμῖν· Ἰδοὺ ὥσπερ ἐφύλαξαν οὗτοι τὴν ἐντολὴν τοῦ Θεοῦ· οὕτως ἐμεγάλυνεν τὸ ἔλεος αὐτοῦ εἰς αὐτοὺς ὁ Κύριος καὶ Θεὸς ἡμῶν.

Διηγήσατο ἡμῖν ὁ Ἀθανάσιος ὁ κατὰ τὸν ζημαρχὸν Ἀντιοχεὺς, λέγων περὶ τοῦ ἀββᾶ Βροχᾶ Αἰγυπτίου ἐν Σελευκείᾳ, πλησίον Ἀντιοχείας· Εὗρεν ἔξωθεν τῆς πόλεως τόπον ἔρημον, καὶ ἐζήτει ποιῆσαι ἑαυτῷ ἐκεῖ μικρὸν κελλίον. Καὶ διοικοδομήσας οὐκ εἶχεν ξύλα στεγάσαι αὐτό. Καὶ εἰσελθὼν ἐν μιᾷ ἐν τῇ πόλει, εὑρίσκει Ἀνατόλιον τὸν ἐπιλεγόμενον τοῦ Κυρτοῦ, κτήτορα ὄντα Σελευκείας, ἔξω τοῦ οἴκου αὐτοῦ καθεζόμενον. Καὶ προσελθὼν αὐτῷ λέγει· Ποίησον ἀγάπην, δός μοι μικρὸν ξύλον, ἵνα στεγάσω τὴν οἰκίαν μου. Ὁ δὲ μελαγχολήσας λέγει αὐτῷ· Ἰδοὺ ξύλον, ἔπαρον, καὶ ἄπελθε· δείξας αὐτῷ μεγάλην καταρτὴν ἣν εἶχεν ἔμπροσθεν τοῦ οἴκου αὐτοῦ κειμένην, ἥτις εἰς πλοῖον ἐποίει μυριάδων εʹ. Ὁ δὲ ἀγκαλισάμενος τὸ κατάρτιον, καὶ κουφίσας αὐτὴν μόνος ἐκ τῆς γῆς, τέθεικεν αὐτὴν εἰς τὸν ὦμον αὐτοῦ· καὶ λαβὼν ἀπῆλθεν εἰς τὸ κελλίον αὐτοῦ. Καὶ καταπλαγεὶς ἐπὶ τῷ παραδόξῳ τοῦ θαύματος ὁ Ἀνατόλιος, ἐχαρίσατο αὐτῷ τὸ τηλικοῦτον ξύλον εἰς τὴν χρείαν αὐτοῦ ἥνπερ ἐβούλετο· ἐξ οὗπερ οὐ μόνον τὴν ῥηθεῖσαν αὐτοῦ κέλλαν ἐστέγασεν, ἀλλὰ καὶ ἄλλα πολλὰ τῷ κατ᾿ αὐτὸν μοναστηρίῳΟ ἐποίησεν πράγματα.

So, seeing your chastity, 3069I was pierced with compunction for you. But go off to such-and-such a place of the city wall, and dig, and take the money you will find there. And keep it, so that you may pay your debts, and much blessing besides. And pray for me, that I too may find mercy.'

"And after a few days the governor came to the city and ordered the robber to be brought out and beheaded. And a day after, the wife says to her husband, 'Dost thou bid me, my lord? I will go off to where the robber told us, and see whether he spoke the truth.' And he said, 'As it seems good to thee, do it.' And she took a small hoe, in the evening, and came to the place and dug, and found a pot covered over, holding the gold. And using it prudently, she paid it out little by little, as though borrowing from this one and that, until she had paid them all; and after that she got her husband out too."

And the one who told us said: "See: just as these kept the commandment of God, so has our Lord and God magnified his mercy upon them."

CHAPTER 190

Athanasius of Antioch, of the household of Zemarchus, told us about abba Brochas the Egyptian, at Seleucia near Antioch: "He found a deserted place outside the city, and sought to make himself a little cell there. And when he had built it up, he had no timber to roof it. And going into the city one day, he finds Anatolius, called 'the Hunchback's', who was a landowner of Seleucia, sitting outside his house. And he went up to him and says, 'Do me a kindness: give me a little timber, so that I may roof my house.' And he, in a black temper, says to him, 'There's timber: lift it and be off', showing him a great mast that he had lying in front of his house, which would serve for a ship of five myriads. Abba Brochas says to him, 'Bless, and I will lift it.' Anatolius, again in a black temper, says to him, 'Blessed be the Lord.' And he put his arms round the mast, and raised it from the ground alone, and set it on his shoulder; and taking it, he went off to his cell.

Ἔλεγεν περὶ τοῦ ἁγίου Ἰωάννου Κωνσταντινουπόλεως, τοῦ δικαίως ἐπονομασθέντος Χρυσοστόμου, διὰ τὸ τοῦ διδασκαλικοῦ λόγου καθαρὸν καὶ διαυγὲς ... καὶ σωτήριον ἔλαβεν βάπτισμα, οὔτε ὤμοσεν, οὔτε ὀμόσαι οὔτε τινὸς κατελάλησεν, οὔτε ἐψεύσατο, οὔτε εὐτράπελα ἐλάλησεν, οὔτε εὐτραπελίαν ἐδέχετο.

They used to say of holy John of Constantinople, who was justly surnamed Chrysostom for the purity and clarity of his teaching word, … [?] [that from the time he] received saving baptism, he neither swore, nor [made anyone] swear [?], nor spoke ill of anyone, nor lied, nor spoke jesting words, nor admitted jesting.

The priest named Peter, who came from Rome, told us about Gregory, among the saints, the pope of that same city: "Having become pope, he built a great monastery of men, and gave a commandment that none of the monks should have anything of his own, not even so much as an obol. Now a brother of the monastery had a brother who was a layman, and he asked him, saying, 'I have no shirt; but do me a kindness: buy me one.' The layman says to him, 'Here are three gold pieces: take them and buy thyself whatever kind pleases thee.' So the monk took the three gold pieces, and went and told the superior; the superior, hearing it, went and reported it to the most holy pope; and the blessed Gregory, learning of it, excommunicated him, as having broken the canons of the monastery. So after a little time the excommunicated brother died, without the pope's learning of it. So after two or three days the superior went and told him that the brother had gone to his rest. At this he was grieved beyond measure, that he had not loosed him from the penalty of excommunication before he departed this life; and writing a prayer on a slip he gave it to one of the archdeacons, bidding him go and read it over the brother. And it was a prayer loosing him, the dead man, from the excommunication. So the archdeacon went, as he had been charged, and read over the brother the letter that had the prayer in it; and that same night the superior sees the brother who had died; and the superior says, 'And where wast thou until today?' And the brother says to him, 'Truly, lord, in prison; and until yesterday I was not released.' So it became known to all that in the very hour the archdeacon said the prayer over the tomb, in that hour he was loosed from the excommunication, and his soul was set free from condemnation."

CHAPTER 193

He told us about abba Apollinarius among the saints, the pope of Alexandria, that he was very merciful and compassionate; and they told this to confirm it: There was in Alexandria a young man, a son born of the first men of the city. When they had died and left much of theirs [?] in ships and in gold, he went astray in managing it, and lost everything, and was driven into the last poverty: not by eating it up, nor by spending it on riotous living, but by falling into various shipwrecks.

Ὧν τελευτησάντων, καὶ πολλὰ τῶν αὐτῶν ἐν πλοίοις καὶ ἐν χρυσίῳ καταλελοιπότων, ἀστοχήσας περὶ τὴν τούτων διοίκησιν, καὶ πάντα ἀπολέσας, καὶ εἰς πενίαν ἐσχάτην ἐλάσας, οὐ φαγὼν, οὐδὲ εἰς ἀσωτείαν δαπανήσας, ἀλλὰ ναυαγίοις διαφόροις περιπεσὼν, καὶ ἀπὸ μεγάλων γεγονὼς μικρὸς, κατὰ τὸ ἐν Ψαλμοῖς εἰρημένον· «Ἀναβαίνουσιν ἕως τῶν οὐρανῶν, καὶ καταβαίνουσιν ἕως τῶν ἀβύσσων·» οὕτως καὶ ὁ νεώτερος πολὺ ἄνω παρέχων ἐν χρήμασι, πολλῷ πλέον κάτω γέγονεν. 3073Τοῦτο ἀκηκοὼς ὁ μακάριος Ἀπολινάριος, καὶ θεωρήσας ἐν τοιαύτῃ ἀσχολίᾳ καὶ πενίᾳ τὸν νεώτερον γενόμενον, γινώσκων δὲ καὶ τοὺς αὐτοῦ γονεῖς, καὶ ἐπιστάμενος ὅπως ἦσαν ἐν πλούτῳ, ἠθέλησεν ἐλεῆσαι αὐτὸν, καὶ δοῦναι μικρὰν ἀγάπην εἰς ἀποστροφὴν, καὶ ᾐσχύνετο. Καὶ καθότι ἐθεώρει αὐτὸν ἐν τῷ σηκρήτῳ αὐτοῦ τὴν ψυχὴν ἐδάκνετο, βλέπων ἐσθῆτα ῥερυπωμένην, καὶ πρόσωπον σκυθρωπὸν ἔχοντα· ἃ τῆς ἐσχάτης πτωχείας καθέστηκε σύμβολα· ὡς οὖν ἦν ἐν τοιαύτῃ φροντίδι ὁ πάπας, ἐν μιᾷ θεόθεν κινηθεὶς, βουλὴν ὄντως θαυμασίαν βουλεύεται, καὶ πρέπουσαν τῇ αὐτοῦ μακαριότητι. Καὶ πέμψας ἤγαγε τὸν νομικὸν τῆς ἁγιωτάτης Ἐκκλησίας· καὶ λαβὼν αὐτὸν κατ᾿ ἰδίαν, λέγει αὐτῷ, Δύνῃ μοι φυλάξαι μυστήριον, κύριε ὦ νομικέ; Ὁ δὲ λέγει αὐτῷ· Ἐλπίζω, δέσποτα, εἰς τὸν Υἱὸν τοῦ Θεοῦ· ὅτι ἐὰν κελεύσῃς, οὐ μὴ ἀνθρώπῳ εἴπω οὐδὲ μάθοι τις ἐξ ἐμοῦ τί ποτε ὧν θαῤῥήσῃς τῷ δούλῳ σου. Τότε λέγει αὐτῷ ὁ πάπας Ἀπολινάριος· Ὕπαγε, ποίησον γραμμάτιον χρεωστικὸν πεντήκοντα χρυσίων λιτρῶν, ὡς ἀπὸ τῆς ἁγιωτάτης Ἐκκλησίας, εἰς τὸν Μακάριον τόνδε τὸν πατέρα τοῦδε τοῦ νεωτέρου, καὶ βάλε, καὶ μάρτυρας, καὶ κομπλατίονα, καὶ αἴρε μοι αὐτὸ τὸ πρόσωπον. Ὁ δὲ νομικὸς τὸ κελευθὲν ἀπὸ τοῦ πάπα εὐθὺς καὶ παραχρῆμα ποιήσας ἤνεγκεν τὸ χειρόγραφον, καὶ δέδωκεν αὐτῷ. Ἐπειδὴ οὖν ὁ πατὴρ τοῦ νεωτέρου εἶχεν χρόνους δέκα ἢ καὶ πλεῖον τελευτήσας, ὁ δὲ χάρτης νέος ἦν, λέγει αὐτῷ ὁ πάπας· Ὕπαγε, κύριε ὦ νομικὲ, σῶσον τὸν χάρτην ἐν σίτῳ ἢ ἐν κριθῇ, καὶ μετ᾿ ὀλί-( γας ἡμέρας φέρε μοι αὐτόν. Ὁ δὲ ἀπελθὼν ἦλθεν μετὰ τὰς ῥηθείσας ἡμέρας φέρων τὸ γραμμάτιον τῷ πάπᾳ. Καὶ σκεψάμενος ὁ νομικὸς λέγει αὐτῷ, ὡς Πρὸ τῶν εʹ ἢ καὶ ἓξ ἡμερῶν ζητῶν χάρτας ἐν τῷ οἴκῳ μου εὗρον τὸ γραμμάτιον τοῦτο, καὶ μέμνημαι, ὅτι ὁ Μακάριος ὁ πατὴρ θαῤῥῶν εἰς ἐμὲ, εἴασέν μοι αὐτὸ πρὸς ὀλίγας ἡμέρας· καὶ τελευτήσαντος αὐτοῦ συνέβη μεῖναι παρ᾿ ἐμοὶ ἕως τῆς σήμερον, καὶ λήθῃ παρέδραμεν, καὶ οὐκ ἦλθεν εἰς νοῦν μου δοῦναί σοι αὐτό.
